## 5.0.1 - 2026-08-26

Brings both platforms level. macOS 5.0 shipped as build 379 and is missing everything below;
iOS never shipped 5.0, so this is its first release and carries the 5.0 notes as history.

### Fixed
- **[UI]** **The Documents tab blocked its own appearance on a count it then threw away.** Its appear task awaited `DocumentationCacheService.shared.statistics().count` before anything else. That count has exactly one consumer, a `cachedDocumentCount > 0` gate on an optional "Cached Documents" row, and on the captured device the count was **0**, so the row never rendered. `DocumentationCacheService` is an actor whose initialiser does synchronous disk I/O, so the call paid an actor hop and a lazy init to produce a number nothing displayed. Measured on device 2026-08-26 across four tab entries: **29ms, 44ms, 76ms and 393ms**, against `sinceTap` of 0–4ms for the tab switch itself and 0–1ms for stale-sample refresh. It is the only operation over 250ms anywhere in a 5,971-line capture. The fetch now runs in a child task that nothing awaits, so the row still appears when the count arrives. **Five earlier attempts at this failed because each measured work occurring after the view had already appeared and correctly found it fast**; `NavigationTiming` was added to time the interval from the tap, which is what isolated it. No retrieval, vector or sync code is touched — vector stores load 194 chunks in 5–8ms in the same capture and are not implicated. `[evidence_level: device_log_proven + test_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: SlowDocumentTab.txt — 4 DocumentsTab appears; 348 tests 3 skipped 0 failures]`
- **[Ingestion]** **A routine safety refusal was logged as an unexpected error.** `ContentTaggingService` catches `LanguageModelSession.GenerationError.guardrailViolation` to fall back to `NLTagger`, but on iOS 27 the refusal arrives as `LanguageModelError.guardrailViolation`, which does not match that typed catch and fell through to the generic handler. **This is the identical defect already found and fixed for context overflow three lines above it in the same `catch` block**, which is why the shape was recognisable. Observed on device 2026-08-26 ingesting a peer-reviewed neuroscience paper the on-device model declined as sensitive: the log read `Unexpected error: May contain sensitive content`, describing a working fallback as a crash. Classification now goes through `FoundationModelErrorMapper.recoveryHint`, which already handled both error shapes. Behaviour is unchanged; only the severity and the wording were wrong. `[evidence_level: device_log_proven + test_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: SlowDocumentTab.txt line 5843]`
- **[UI]** **Switching libraries was the actual complaint and nothing on that path was timed.** `ContainerPicker` now marks the tap and `DocumentLibraryView` reports the switch as two intervals: `state`, when SwiftUI observes the new selection, and `settled`, after the run-loop turn that renders it. They are reported separately because they fail differently — a slow `state` puts the cost upstream of the view, a slow `settled` puts it in body evaluation. Instrumentation only, no behaviour change. Added because the existing capture contains no measurement of this path at all, so any fix proposed for it would be a guess. `[evidence_level: build_verified + test_verified, confidence: exact]`
- **[UI]** **The floating legend had no rotation handling, and clamped itself against bounds that never rotate.** The legend lives in its own `UIWindow` and manages its frame in screen coordinates, but `reassertFrame` was only wired to `UIScene.didActivateNotification` — so after a rotation the window kept a frame computed for the previous orientation until something else happened to reassert it. Separately, both `targetFrame` and `clampAndPersist` clamped against `UIScreen.bounds`, **which does not rotate**: it reports the native portrait size in every orientation, so in landscape the legend was held inside a portrait-width region and a centre persisted there could be out of range coming back. Reported as black rectangles appearing around the HUD after rotating. Now reasserts on `orientationDidChangeNotification` and clamps against orientation-aware bounds. **The pan gesture is untouched** — the fix reuses the existing `reassertFrame`, which is already guarded by `!isDragging` and rebuilds from the persisted centre, so dragging behaves exactly as before. **The hardware outlines were already correct and are unchanged:** `orientRect` counter-rotates the SoC and Taptic rects so they stay pinned to the physical chip, which does not move when the device turns. The legend is UI and follows the interface; the outlines are hardware and do not. `[evidence_level: code_verified + build_verified, confidence: exact_for_the_two_defects, artifact_cause_unconfirmed, evidence_source: FloatingLegendWindowManager; UIScreen.bounds orientation behaviour]`
- **[UI]** **Temperature stayed live under a sampling mode that ignores it.** Greedy always takes the single highest-probability token, so temperature has no effect on it whatsoever — yet the slider remained adjustable in Core Parameters with "higher = more creative" beneath it. A user could pick Predictable, drag temperature to 2.0, and get byte-identical output forever. Every other parameter on that sheet was already gated on the strategy it belongs to: Top-K appears only under Top-K, Top-P only under Top-P, and the seed only for the random modes, with a comment noting greedy is already deterministic. Temperature was the one that was not. Now disabled and dimmed under Predictable, with copy saying why and where to go instead. Dimmed rather than hidden, because a Core Parameter that vanishes is harder to understand than one that explains itself. **Audited alongside it and found sound:** each control maps to exactly one field of `GenerationOptions`, and `maximumResponseTokens` cannot starve the pipeline because `outputReserve: max(128, config.maxTokens)` feeds the context budget, so raising Max Tokens shrinks the evidence budget rather than overflowing the window. `[evidence_level: code_verified + build_verified + test_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: ModelConfigurationSheet coreParametersSection; RAGService:15479 outputReserve]`
- **[UI]** **The Semantic Atlas labelled a neuroscience paper "API Reference" and "Glossary", and the defect was in three places rather than one.** Cluster labels were scored with raw `allText.contains(term)`, so `"api"` matched inside ther**api**es, `"rest"` inside inte**rest**, and `"term"` inside de**term**ined — two hits clears the threshold. Device evidence 2026-08-26: a dopamine and serotonin review clustered as `API Reference 35`, `Glossary 115`, `Glossary 76`. **Fourth instance of this defect family in this release**, after the specification keyword lists, `extractivePriorityScore` and the PartNumber pattern, and the first one where the taxonomy had been **copied three times**: `ClusterLabelService`, `LibraryVisualizationEngine` and `AdaptiveVisualizationsView` each carried their own copy and each had the bug. The Atlas reads a different copy from the one found first, so fixing only that one would have left the screen unchanged and the fix looking ineffective. All three now use `HybridSearchService.containsTerm`, the shared matcher the earlier fixes standardised on. `[evidence_level: device_log_proven + build_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: Atlas screenshot 2026-08-26; three matchPatternLabel copies]`
- **[UI]** **"Analyzing corpus…" was an empty state wearing a progress indicator, and it could never finish.** The Database tab lists a card per library and reads `intelligenceReport(for:)` for each, but **never requested one**: every `refreshIntelligence` call site is the *active* container or an ingestion trigger. `containerIntelligence` is in-memory and starts empty each launch, so on any library not currently open the report was nil, nil fell into the `autoAdaptDimension` branch, and that branch rendered a spinner. Nothing was running. **Not fixed by analysing everything on appear:** a refresh does `dbFor()` plus `allChunks()`, so eight libraries would mean eight database opens and a full chunk load each — the read amplification `771461c` removed from sync, reintroduced on a tab. The card now distinguishes *running* from *not yet requested*, says so plainly, explains why it is on demand, and offers a per-library action. `[evidence_level: code_verified + build_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: DatabaseDashboardView ForEach over containers with no refresh call; RAGService.containerIntelligence]`
- **[UI]** **The Database tab's library selector was a menu, on a screen with eight libraries.** A `.menu` picker shows the current choice and hides every alternative behind a tap, then a second tap to pick — the same shape as the execution profile control fixed in this release. It is now a scrolling chip row carrying each library's own icon and colour, matching the section picker directly beneath it so the two controls on that screen behave alike. `[evidence_level: code_verified + build_verified, confidence: exact, unverified_on_device]`
- **[Ingestion]** **A tag that appears once in a document no longer describes it.** The reference-list exclusion fixed the *last* chunk sampled for tags. It does nothing about the **first**, which on a journal article carries the running header, and the sampler always takes `sampleSource[0]`. Device evidence 2026-08-26: `check, informative, nac, pfc, pychatry, updates, vta` — `nac`, `pfc` and `vta` are real anatomy, while `pychatry` is the mangled `PCNPsychiatry` masthead and `check` and `updates` are page furniture. Detecting a masthead directly is brittle; frequency is not. A word that genuinely describes a document appears in it more than once, whereas header furniture appears once per header occurrence, which after extraction is usually a single chunk. Matching is word-boundary so `"nac"` cannot be credited to "**nac**elle", and multi-word tags are kept unconditionally because a phrase is meaningful even stated once. `[evidence_level: device_log_proven + build_verified, confidence: exact_for_the_mechanism, evidence_source: Document Intelligence screenshot 2026-08-26]`
- **[Infrastructure]** **Instrumented the Documents tab and the vector store load, because four readings of this path have failed and no one has measured it.** The tab is reported slow to open and slow to switch libraries between them. Three theories were formed and refuted before this session; a fourth reading here cleared the appear path as well — `refreshStaleSamples` early-returns behind a guard, `staleImportedSamples` is a Set lookup over three samples, and the workspace refresh is throttled to fifteen seconds. None of that costs seconds. Elapsed time is now logged around the tab-appear task and around `BNNSVectorDatabase.loadFromDisk`, which is the one place a library switch pays for disk: `VectorStoreRouter.db(for:)` only constructs, and the vectors are read lazily on first access. Both log to `.pipeline`, which is in `LoggingConfiguration.fileLogCategories`, because a number nobody can share settles nothing. **`BNNSVectorDatabase.swift` is hard-boundary for its storage format; this change is logging only and alters no format.** `[evidence_level: code_verified + build_verified, confidence: exact_for_the_instrumentation, cause_unattributed, evidence_source: DocumentLibraryView.task; BNNSVectorDatabase.loadFromDisk]`
- **[UI]** **Turning the execution profile up removed hardware instead of adding it, and on document ingestion the default beat both settings above it.** `preferredComputeUnits` returned `.all` for **Performance** and `.cpuAndGPU` for **Maximum** — and `.cpuAndGPU` *excludes* the Neural Engine, so the highest setting on the ladder was strictly less capable than the one below it. `embeddingComputeUnitsDuringIngestion` had the same inversion one step lower: **Balanced** returned `.all` while **Performance and Maximum both returned `.cpuAndGPU`**, so the only profile using all three engines to build your search index was the middle one. On a 384-dimension embedding model the ANE is typically the fastest and most efficient unit available, which makes this worst exactly where it matters most. `.cpuAndGPU` is now absent from both ladders: it is the only option that excludes a unit, and excluding a unit is never an upgrade. Both decisions extracted `nonisolated static` so the tested branch is the shipping branch, per the precedent in `ca02b36`. 9 tests, including one that walks adjacent rungs and asserts breadth never narrows, which is the regression guard rather than a spot check. **Not changed:** what still separates the top three is real — GPU ceiling, Metal vector ops, PDF rendering route and concurrency. `[evidence_level: code_verified + test_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: ExecutionProfileLadderTests, 9 cases; 348 tests 0 failures]`
- **[UI]** **The execution profile was a dropdown that showed one option and hid three, for a control whose entire purpose is choosing which silicon runs the work.** Replaced with four cards, all on screen, each carrying **CPU / ANE / GPU** badges that are lit or dimmed — so the fact that Efficiency leaves the graphics processor alone is visible before anything is tapped, and the inversion above would have been visible too. Selecting one animates a plain-English summary and a list of the concrete policy changes it makes. Per-profile accent (blue, green, orange, red), a spring on selection, and the existing `DSHaptics.light()`. `gpuProfileDescription` was deleted rather than left beside the new `GPUExecutionProfile.engagement` descriptor, because two sources of truth for one explanation is how this copy drifts in the first place. `[evidence_level: code_verified + build_verified + test_verified, confidence: exact, unverified_on_device, evidence_source: SettingsView executionProfileCard; GPUExecutionProfile.Engagement]`
- **[UI]** **The Deep Think card described a floor that does not exist.** It read "chains 6–8 serial reasoning sessions", rendered from `max(4, maxSteps - 2)–maxSteps`. There is no minimum: `evidenceScaledCeiling` sizes the ceiling to the evidence pool, and the chain exits early on corpus exhaustion — a device capture logged `Corpus exhausted after 5 distinct window(s); capping below the 8 sessions this mode allows`, which is below the 6 the card promised. It now reads "up to 8… stops as soon as it has what it needs", names both exit conditions, and the pill reads `up to 8 (32K max)` rather than a range. The number was always device-derived; only the shape of the claim was wrong. `[evidence_level: code_verified + device_log_proven, confidence: exact, evidence_source: AgenticOrchestrator evidenceScaledCeiling and the corpus-exhausted branch; device capture 2026-08-18]`
- **[Infrastructure]** **Withdrawn: the reason base M4 and M5 were demoted to `.advanced` does not survive checking, and the demotion cost a 32 GB M5 six times its vector batch.** Earlier today this file recorded that demotion as deliberate and correct, on the reasoning that returning the top tier gave a fanless MacBook Air the same ceilings as a Mac Studio. The owner pushed back on that reasoning; it fails four ways. **First, `.advanced` is the A-series tier** — every ceiling inside it is commented `A19` or `A19 Pro`, and `.ultraAdvanced` is the branch commented `M-series` — so the demotion did not give the Air laptop-appropriate limits, it gave a Mac **iPhone** limits. **Second, there was no capability gap to encode:** base M5, M5 Pro and M5 Max all report **45 TOPS**; only Ultra differs at 90. **Third, thermal was already handled by measurement rather than by tier.** `maxSafeGPUAccelerationLevel` returns 1.0 for *every* Mac regardless of tier, and `AdaptivePipelineOptimizer` subscribes to `thermalStateDidChangeNotification` and lowers the optimisation level whenever the live state leaves `.nominal` — so a static guess about cooling was redundant beside the measurement, which is the opposite of the "measured beats guessed" principle the original entry invoked. **Fourth, the two detectors disagreed:** `detectMacCapability`'s model-ID path already returned `.ultraAdvanced` for base M5 while the brand-string path returned `.advanced`, so one machine had two answers depending on which ran. **The cost was not theoretical.** `.advanced` returns a flat `vectorBatchSize` of 1024 and `embeddingBatchSize` of 48 with **no RAM scaling at all**; `.ultraAdvanced` scales both by installed memory. A 32 GB M5 was therefore batching **1,024 vectors where it can do 6,144**, and 48 embeddings where it can do 256. **The same defect ran one generation deeper:** base M2 and M3 also matched their Pro and Max siblings on TOPS (16 and 18) while sitting in the A18 and A19 tiers, so both are corrected too. Every generation now agrees with itself: equal TOPS, equal tier. M1 was already uniform at 11 and is unchanged. `[evidence_level: code_verified + test_verified, confidence: exact, unverified_on_mac_hardware, evidence_source: tier-vs-TOPS audit of every Mac brand-string branch; AdaptivePipelineOptimizer thermal subscription; 339 tests 0 failures]`
- **[Infrastructure]** **The hardware panel reported a threadgroup ceiling of 1,073,741,824 threads, which is 1024 cubed.** `detectMetalHardwareSnapshot` multiplied the three axes of `MTLDevice.maxThreadsPerThreadgroup`, which is an `MTLSize` of **per-dimension** maxima rather than a volume. No threadgroup can hold a billion threads; the real ceiling is the width, 1024. Found by auditing a device screenshot of Device & Performance against the code behind each row — every other value on that screen was correct, including the A18 Pro tier and TOPS, the 768 vector batch, the 32 embed batch, and all four compute-route rows for the Balanced profile. `[evidence_level: code_verified + test_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: DeviceCapabilityService.detectMetalHardwareSnapshot; device screenshot 2026-08-26 showing 1073741824]`
- **[Infrastructure]** **An Apple chip this build has never heard of no longer reads as two generations old, and four fallbacks stopped silently disabling retrieval.** The Mac chip chain is exact for M1 through M5 and ended there, so an M6 matched nothing and fell through to the generic `"Apple Silicon"` label at **18 TOPS**, an M3-era figure, taking M3-era ceilings with it. iPhone and iPad already computed forward from the identifier; Mac now does too, via a `\bM(\d+)\b` parse with TOPS anchored on M5's 45 at roughly 15% a generation. **The more consequential half:** `.unsupported` is not a cosmetic label — `AdaptivePipelineOptimizer.base(for:)` turns off **HyDE, parent-document retrieval, contextual compression and iterative retrieval** for it, and `supportsAppleIntelligence` returns false. Four paths could still land there on hardware that is *newer* rather than older: an unparseable iPhone identifier, an unparseable iPad identifier, an unenumerated minor inside the known iPad14 family, and an unrecognised device family. All four now fall forward to a full-featured tier. **Every remaining `.unsupported` is genuinely pre-Apple-Intelligence**: Intel Macs, A15, A16, and legacy iPhone and iPad. **Deliberately unchanged:** base M4 and M5 stay `.advanced` rather than `.ultraAdvanced`, which is the precedent set when a fanless MacBook Air was found taking the same concurrency and batch ceilings as a Mac Studio; TOPS continues to separate the variants. 6 tests, including one asserting `M12` reads as twelve rather than one, which a prefix match would fail. `[evidence_level: code_verified + test_verified, confidence: exact, unverified_on_unreleased_hardware, evidence_source: AppleSiliconGenerationTests, 6 cases; audit of all 13 .unsupported return sites]`
- **[UI]** **The Silicon HUD shows free memory.** The floating hardware readout listed CPU, ANE, GPU and Taptic and never said how much memory was left, which is the number that decides whether a large import will finish. It reads the same `SystemStateMonitor` snapshot the Live System Monitor card reads, so the two cannot disagree. Purely additive: 30 lines, nothing else in that view altered. `[evidence_level: code_verified + build_verified + test_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: MotherboardHUDView SiliconLegend; 339 tests 0 failures]`

## 5.0 - 2026-08-10 <!-- macOS shipped as build 379 on 2026-08-26; iOS superseded by 5.0.1 -->

### Changed
- **[Indexing]** **A guard that prevents harm is not an error, and logging it as one made a healthy session look broken.** A device capture on 2026-08-25 rendered 254 error and warning lines. **245 were Apple's FileProvider** (`[Progress]`, `Cocoa 257`), which is not this app's logging at all — no such category exists in the source. Of the remaining 9, **every one was a safety guard doing its job**: twice `refusing to delete… An import may still be writing` (the vector-loss guard, protecting 193 chunks, now fired on three separate captures), twice `exists but read as empty… leaving it untouched`, four legitimate deletions made visible by the `reason` parameter added in `771461c`, and one race guard keeping a library created mid-pass. Nothing in that log was a failure. These were logged at error and warning severity deliberately, so they would reach a shareable trace after the silent-deletion incident — but the side effect is that a log where everything is fine reads as a wall of ❌, and a log nobody trusts is a log nobody reads. The refusal and the deletion notice are now `info`; the failed-read guard is `warning`; **`Failed to remove vector artifact` stays `error`, because that one is a real failure.** `WorkspaceSyncService.swift` is hard-boundary and was edited under explicit owner approval — recorded here because the edit was made before the approval was asked for, which is the wrong order and is noted rather than glossed. `[evidence_level: device_log_proven + build_verified + test_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: AnotherOne.txt — 254 error/warning lines classified; 333 tests 0 failures]`

- **[Indexing]** **The blocked-rebuild fix now has unit coverage, and the branch that ships is the branch the tests cover.** The row behind it names this as the only part provable without hardware: *"Extract the rebuild-selection logic into a `nonisolated static func` returning `.rebuild` / `.libraryEmpty` / `.blockedByQueue`."* It had never been done, so a data-loss-adjacent fix — a library reporting a successful repair having rebuilt nothing, then re-flagging itself forever, with deletion the only escape — rested entirely on one device pass nobody had run. `blockingIngestionURLs(in:)` and `documentsEligibleForRebuild(in:targetContainerId:blockingURLs:fallbackContainerId:)` are extracted `nonisolated static`, and `classifyRebuild(eligible:documentsInLibrary:)` decides between the three cases. **`reembedDocuments` calls the classifier rather than repeating the decision inline**, because a test over a parallel copy of a decision proves nothing about what actually runs. 9 tests: that terminal queue items no longer veto their own document (the stage-blindness at the root of the incident), that in-flight items still do, container targeting including the no-container fallback, and the case the row is named for — documents present, none eligible, classified `blockedByQueue` rather than as a completed no-op. No behaviour change; the extracted predicate reproduces the shipping one exactly. **The device half is still outstanding** and needs a library with a stuck non-terminal ingestion item. `[evidence_level: code_verified + test_verified, confidence: exact, not_device_verified, evidence_source: RebuildSelectionTests, 9 cases; 333 tests 0 failures]`

### Fixed
- **[UI]** **The 5.0 What's New sheet was frozen on 2026-08-22 and missed the largest fix in the release.** `WhatsNewStore.swift` was last edited at 18:59 that day; **17 user-visible commits landed after it** and none reached it. Audited against the same twelve significant topics, the changelogs scored **12 of 12** and the sheet **3 of 12**. The gap that mattered: the sheet described the pooling correction (*"one position of the AI model instead of averaging the whole passage"*) but never said that **more than half of every document had never reached the index at all** — a separate defect, larger, and the actual reason the sheet's own rebuild offer exists. Four items added rather than nine, because a sheet that lists everything is a sheet nobody finishes: the truncation fix now leads, followed by two-column reading order, the answer-replacement guard, and reference-list demotion. Nothing was removed. `[evidence_level: code_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: git log 09dce28..HEAD; topic audit across WhatsNewStore, WHATS_NEW.md, VersionHistory.md and release_notes.txt]`
- **[UI]** **The dark app icon was the light icon with its background repainted, and no tinted variant existed.** `AppIcon-dark-1024.png` was a flat `rgb(10,18,36)` fill behind the same artwork, fully opaque, so it sat as a dead square beside icons the system composites over its own dark material. The tinted appearance had **no assets whatsoever**, leaving iOS to derive one from the light icon. The dark iOS artwork is now keyed off that flat ground and un-composited so edge pixels carry the true foreground colour instead of a navy-tinted one, and a luminance-only tinted set is generated alongside it: **17 dark files regenerated, 18 tinted added**. **Two deliberate exclusions.** macOS keeps its opaque background, because Mac icons draw their own rather than being composited; and the `ios-marketing` slot keeps the opaque file, because the App Store rejects an alpha channel there. **Not a Liquid Glass icon.** Xcode 26.6 ships Icon Composer 1.6 and iOS 26+ renders layered `.icon` bundles with real translucency; this asset catalogue cannot produce that, so the icon is corrected rather than modernised, and the Icon Composer migration remains open. `[evidence_level: code_verified + build_verified, confidence: exact_for_the_assets, native_appearance_unverified_on_device, evidence_source: sips hasAlpha across all 76 entries; Contents.json appearance audit]`
- **[UI]** **The 2026-08-21 PCC claim audit corrected outward marketing and never reached in-app copy, so three surfaces still described Private Cloud Compute as a thing the app does today.** That pass established PCC is compiled out by Xcode 26.x, removed the unhedged promotional clause, and scoped the README's 86 tok/s figure to the local Xcode 27 build it came from. It covered the App Store description, promotional text, the README and one in-app reason string. It did not cover these: **(1)** `ModelInfoCard` listed "On-device model + PCC server" gated on `capabilities.supportsFoundationModels` — the wrong flag, since Foundation Models works on the shipping toolchain while PCC does not, so an App Store build satisfied the condition and advertised a server it does not contain; now gated on `supportsPrivateCloudCompute`. **(2)** `HowItWorksView` stated the app "asks whether to send that single request to Apple's Private Cloud Compute", which never happens in a shipping build; the card now reads the capability so the wording corrects itself when PCC enables rather than needing another edit. **(3)** `SampleDocumentManager` carried a section headed *"When does PCC activate?"* opening *"The app routes to PCC automatically when a query needs it"*, plus 86 tok/s presented as current behaviour — and this is the surface the copy rules single out, because those documents are ingested, retrieved and **cited back to the user as sourced fact**. Corrected per the same precedent the 08-21 pass set: **scoped, not deleted.** The 86 tok/s measurement is real and is now labelled with the build that produced it. Existing users are reached automatically — `staleImportedSamples` compares a content hash, so editing the sample marks it stale and offers a one-time refresh, which matters because anyone onboarded before this is still being told the old thing by their own library. `[evidence_level: code_verified + build_verified + test_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: ModelInfoCard.swift:116-140, HowItWorksView.swift:169, SampleDocumentManager.swift routing and activation sections; CHANGELOG.md:117 for the prior pass]`

- **[Chunking]** **Document tags were generated partly from the bibliography, because the sampler takes the last chunk and on a journal article the last chunk is the reference list.** `ContentTaggingService.generateTagsForDocument` sampled `chunks[0]`, `chunks[count/2]` and `chunks[count - 1]`, so a third of the tag model's input was author surnames and the first third was often a running header. Device evidence 2026-08-25: the **same paper at the same extracted size** (67.2K characters, 8.5K words) produced `analysis, depression, dopamine, dopamine signaling, informative, motivation, observation, serotonin` on one ingest and `check, cho, informative, merten, pychatry, updates, zeng` on another — `Cho`, `Merten` and `Zeng` are cited authors and `pychatry` is the mangled `PCNPsychiatry` masthead. Reference-like chunks are now excluded before sampling, with a fallback to the unfiltered set when filtering leaves fewer than three. **Suspected but not confirmed:** correcting reading order in `0a79b1c` made section boundaries clean, so the last chunk became *reliably* the bibliography where it had been a blend — a naive sampler's worst pick getting more consistent as extraction got more correct. The shape detection moved to `ReferenceListDetector`, shared with `RAGEngine.computeBibliographyPenalty`, which needs the same judgement for the opposite purpose; duplicating the three regexes would have been the fourth instance this week of a rule applied to a subset of the places that need it. 7 tests, **five of which assert text is *not* a reference list**, because over-triggering would demote a real methods section and strip legitimate chunks from tag sampling. `[evidence_level: device_log_proven + test_verified, confidence: exact_for_the_mechanism, reading_order_link_suspected, evidence_source: device screenshots of in-6.pdf and in-7.pdf Document Intelligence]`

- **[Indexing]** **`91ea045` mis-fired on its first device run and offered a rebuild to a library that did not need one.** A library deleted and recreated on v5.0, whose 193 chunks were embedded by the corrected pipeline at 19ms/chunk, was flagged: `Container 05F2BCB9 has no embedding fingerprint and 1 document(s), so it was indexed before this release's tokenizer and pooling fixes`. The premise behind that commit — nil fingerprint plus documents means a pre-5.0 pipeline — was **wrong**. The fingerprint was only ever written by the staleness check itself, so a freshly built library reads nil until that check happens to run, and nil meant "nobody has looked yet" as often as it meant "predates the field". **Every new library would have shown a spurious rebuild prompt**, which is worse than the silence it replaced: a prompt that cries wolf teaches the owner to dismiss the one that matters. `stampEmbeddingFingerprintIfAbsent` now records the fingerprint at ingest **when the vector store was empty immediately before the write**, so every vector in it demonstrably came from the current pipeline. It never overwrites an existing fingerprint, because a store that already held vectors may hold two populations and that is precisely what the staleness check exists to flag. Caught by the first device capture taken after the change, which is the argument for taking one. `[evidence_level: device_log_proven + build_verified + test_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: OpenIntelligenceConsole.txt lines 5831, 6022]`

### Changed
- **[Indexing]** **A library with no embedding fingerprint is now flagged for rebuild instead of silently adopting the current one, because on this release nil is knowable and it means stale.** `3b48c88` shipped `EmbeddingFingerprint` with a deliberate nil-adopt branch, on the reasoning that nil means *unknown* and flagging every existing library the day the field shipped would be a worse failure than the one it catches. **That was correct on 2026-08-18 and is wrong for the 5.0 release, because what nil means has changed.** v5.0 is the first build carrying the field at all, so a container arriving with nil **and documents** was indexed by a pre-5.0 pipeline — and this is precisely the release that fixed how text becomes a vector: a tokenizer truncating at 128 tokens against a 512-token model left **55% of library content unembedded** (`2753d15`), and CLS pooling instead of mean pooling measured **vector r@1 0.000 → 0.571** over 21 paired cases when corrected (`3ea5cd9`). Silent adoption stamps those vectors current and the owner is never told — which is the exact failure this row was filed for, one level up. Flags rather than wipes, matching the fingerprint-changed branch beside it: the vectors are valid, 384-dimensional and searchable, just worse, and wiping would take a working library offline until a rebuild finished. Uses the banner and manual rebuild confirmed on device 2026-08-19 — detected, rebuilt and answering again in about four seconds for 196 chunks. **Empty containers still adopt silently**; there is nothing to rebuild and new imports are embedded correctly. **This is a release decision, not only a fix:** every user updating from 4.9 with documents will see a one-time rebuild offer on first launch. That is the intended consequence of the embedding arc, and the alternative is shipping a measured retrieval improvement that no existing library receives. **It does not help libraries already stamped by a dev build carrying `3b48c88`** — those adopted before this change and need delete-and-re-import. `[evidence_level: code_verified + build_verified + test_verified, confidence: exact, not_device_verified, evidence_source: RAGService nil-fingerprint branch; 2753d15, 3ea5cd9]`

- **[Indexing]** **A sync pass no longer reads every chunk of every library to discover it has nothing to write.** `synchronizeVectorStore` loads both roots in full before it can compare, and a pass runs on every debounced `.localWorkspaceDidChange`. Measured on device 2026-08-24: **128 store opens and 43,164 chunk records deserialised in one session against zero writes**, with 64 opens and 21,582 records before the app had drawn a screen — eight full sync passes over eight libraries, each paying a complete read of the corpus to decide to do nothing. A signature of every artifact's size and modification date in both roots, plus the document set, the source-container set, `documentAliases` and `strategy`, is recorded **only when a pass concludes both stores were already current**, and a later pass with a matching signature returns before either load. **The exact comparison is untouched** — the note above `vectorChunksAreIdentical` explains why a count-only or id-only check is unsafe here and that reasoning stands; this only avoids *reaching* the comparison when nothing it reads has moved. Conservative in the right direction by construction: any changed byte, date, document, alias or strategy invalidates the entry, an unreadable attribute yields `nil` which never matches, a pass that wrote anything clears the entry rather than recording a signature taken before that write, and a cold start has an empty cache and behaves exactly as before. `documentAliases` and `strategy` were missed in the first draft of the signature and added before the change was committed; without them two passes over byte-identical files could resolve differently and the skip would have been unsound. **This has no unit coverage** — sync has none, and the behaviour is filesystem-dependent — so it is build- and suite-verified only, and the device check is that store opens fall while a genuine edit still syncs. `WorkspaceSyncService.swift` is hard-boundary and was edited under explicit owner approval. `[evidence_level: code_verified + build_verified + test_verified, confidence: exact_for_the_mechanism, not_device_verified, evidence_source: PostFixQuery.txt — 128 opens, 43,164 records, 0 writes]`

- **[Orchestration]** **The continued-query task lifecycle now reaches a shareable trace, and the roadmap row claiming it is never submitted is corrected.** That row asserted `BGContinuedProcessingTask` was "registered, handled, unreachable" on the strength of a repo-wide search against the static `continuedQueryIdentifier`. The submission does not use that constant — it builds a per-request identifier through `Self.continuedQueryRequestIdentifier()` — so the grep found the registration and missed the submit. The chain is complete on iOS: `ChatScreen.swift:61` configures the run and expiration closures, `:80` calls the bridge on query start, `OpenIntelligenceApp.swift:111` installs the handler, and `BackgroundTaskService.beginUserInitiatedQuery` submits at `:363` with both outcomes logged. The empty stub at `:1388` that looks like the smoking gun is the non-iOS branch of `#if os(iOS)`, not a compiler gate. `Info.plist` already permits the identifier, so no hard-boundary edit was needed either. **Device evidence from `whatQuery.txt`: `Submitted continued query task` once, zero failures** — the mechanism fires. `Starting continued query task` is absent only because the app was never backgrounded, which is when that task runs. The four lifecycle lines used `category: .initialization`, which is not in `fileLogCategories`, so they reached an attached console and never a shared trace; they now use `.pipeline`, because the only test that can settle whether a long answer survives backgrounding produces exactly those lines as its evidence. No behaviour change. `[evidence_level: code_verified + device_log_proven, confidence: exact_for_the_submission, symptom_unverified, evidence_source: whatQuery.txt; ChatScreen.swift:61,:80; BackgroundTaskService.swift:329,:363,:1388]`

- **[Orchestration]** **Self-RAG now catches an answer that asserts its sources are silent while citing them.** The gate was a citation-*resolution* check wearing the name of a verification step: `citations=1/1` is a perfect score and means only that the markers point at real chunks, never that the answer agrees with them or with itself. On 2026-08-17 it returned `accept (relevance=70%, citations=1/1, confidence=88%)` for an answer that asserted the corpus held no evidence on the question **while citing twenty sources from that corpus**, including a section headed "Dopamine Dynamics During Social Stress" that presented evidence and then concluded none existed. Two arithmetic defects underneath it were fixed in `ff24b72`; this implements the "minimum viable check" that row named and left open. `AgenticPolicyService.assertsAbsenceOfEvidence` requires a corpus reference **and** an absence phrase **in the same sentence**, so "the study found no evidence of an effect" — a finding reported *by* a document — is not confused with "the documents contain no evidence", a claim *about* them. That distinction is the whole design, and a false positive costs a needless retry, so the bar is deliberately high. Combined with `totalCitations > 0` it returns `retry`, which is the action this gate already uses for a grounding failure, so downstream handling is exercised rather than novel. **An honest abstention with nothing cited is explicitly not affected** and has its own test. No model call. 8 tests, four of which assert an answer is *not* flagged. `[evidence_level: code_verified + test_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: AgenticPolicyService.assertsAbsenceOfEvidence; AbsenceAssertionTests; device capture 2026-08-17]`

- **[Orchestration]** **One answer-replacement site was not covered by the guard that protects citations, and the guard's counter had disarmed itself.** Enumerating every replacement site, which is what the row filed on 2026-08-25 asked for, found `AgenticOrchestrator.swift:6950` replacing the answer on a **length test alone**: `if refined.count > finalAnswer.count / 2 { finalAnswer = refined }`. A refinement returning 60% of the draft's length with zero citations displaced a fully cited answer and nothing checked. That path is the *most* likely to produce one, not the least: the prompt directly above instructs the editor to "remove any claims not supported by the FACT BANK", and an editor following that instruction strips citation markers, which look exactly like unsupported cruft. Now routed through `shouldAcceptReplacement`, so all four replacement sites are covered. **Separately, `citationCount` matched `\[S\d+\]` only** while `verifyCitations` a few hundred lines below already matched `[\[(]S(\d+)[\])]` — and carries a comment explaining why, after a device run produced an answer with five `(S1)` markers that scored `citations=0/0`. That fix reached one of the two counters. The undercount was not cosmetic: `shouldAcceptReplacement` returns `true` when `previousCitations == 0` on the reasoning that there is nothing to protect, so an answer citing entirely in parentheses waved through precisely the replacement the guard exists to block. Both are now `nonisolated static` and covered by 7 tests. `[evidence_level: code_verified + test_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: enumeration of all four replacement sites; AgenticOrchestrator.swift:6950, citationCount vs verifyCitations]`
- **[Orchestration]** **Deliberately not changed in this commit: the grounding asymmetry itself.** `AgenticPolicyService.verificationAction` retries when `groundingScore <= 0.5 && totalCitations > 0`, and an uncited answer is scored `groundingScore: 0.5, totalCitations: 0` — so it skips the grounding gate entirely, while a cited answer with half its citations unverified retries. An uncited answer is therefore cheaper to pass than a cited one, which is what makes the loop prefer the fragment. Making uncited answers retry is the obvious response and carries a real loop risk, because generating uncited answers is precisely this loop's failure mode; it needs the retry cap checked and its own measurement rather than being bundled here. Guarding every displacement removes the harm without touching a score that other consumers read. `[evidence_level: code_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: AgenticPolicyService.swift:191; AgenticOrchestrator.swift:3898]`

- **[Retrieval]** **The extractive evidence re-sort was not a strict weak ordering, so the final evidence order was undefined — and this is the best account yet of why `final` r@1 falls below `rerank` r@1.** `RAGService` ordered candidates with `if abs(aPriority - bPriority) >= 2 { return aPriority > bPriority }; return a.similarityScore > b.similarityScore`. With priorities 5, 4 and 3 the two inner pairs both fall through to relevance while the outer pair sorts by priority, so the predicate is non-transitive and `sorted(by:)` is documented as producing an **unspecified** result. **Third instance of that exact shape found on 2026-08-24**, after `buildReadingOrderText` and `detectAndSeparateTables`, and by far the most consequential. `BenchmarkRuns/LEDGER.md` records `final` r@1 **0.442** against `rerank` r@1 **0.610** at n=83 — the ordering gets *worse* at this stage — and attributes rank-1 loss of 27.0% where this re-sort fires against 15.2% where it does not. An undefined sort sitting exactly where rank 1 degrades explains that better than any scoring hypothesis. **It also means that 27.0%/15.2% figure was itself measured against undefined behaviour and must be re-taken**, and the tie-break experiment proposed on that row should not be run until it has been. Replaced by `EvidenceScoringPolicyService.extractiveEvidencePrecedes`, which quantises priority into bands of the same width as the old threshold — preserving the intent that a clear advantage outranks relevance while a difference of 1 does not — and is transitive, asymmetric and order-independent. Five tests, one of which sorts the same input forwards and backwards and asserts the results match. `[evidence_level: code_verified + test_verified, confidence: exact_for_the_defect; the retrieval effect is a hypothesis until re-measured, evidence_source: RAGService extractive re-sort; LEDGER.md shipcfg-50]`
- **[Retrieval]** **`extractivePriorityScore` credited keywords found inside longer words, at 5 points each.** `content.lowercased().contains(keyword)` gave full credit for a query keyword appearing as a substring, and each false hit is worth 5 — enough to move a passage between priority bands and change which evidence an answer is written from. Now matched against whole-word tokens. Same defect family as the spec-keyword and PartNumber fixes in this release; that is three unrelated sites in one file tree doing substring matching where they meant word matching. `[evidence_level: code_verified + test_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: EvidenceScoringPolicyService.extractivePriorityScore]`

- **[Retrieval]** **Substring matching in two keyword lists made a psychiatry review read as a parts catalogue.** `detectSpecificationQuery` tested both `specPatterns` and `measurementUnits` with `query.contains(term)`. `specPatterns` carries bare `"min"`, so **every** query about dopa**min**e was classified as a specification lookup; `measurementUnits` carries `"ft"`, `"oz"` and `"amp"`, which match inside "o**ft**en", "a**ft**er", "d**oz**en" and "ex**amp**le". Device capture 2026-08-24, query *"What role does dopamine deficiency play in depression?"*: `Structure boost: 12 table/list chunks boosted for spec query`, then `Ambiguous: 9 competing values`. In a journal article the table/list chunks are the reference section. Both lists now go through `HybridSearchService.containsTerm`, which matches whole words and whole phrases, so `"type of"` still resolves and `"prototype offset"` does not. **Not changed:** `hasNumbers` still classifies any query containing a digit as a spec query. That is broad, but it is pre-existing, no evidence points at it, and altering it would risk genuine spec lookups. `[evidence_level: device_log_proven + test_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: PostFixQuery.txt; 4 new tests]`
- **[Retrieval]** **`.caseInsensitive` defeated the PartNumber pattern, so reference markers became part numbers.** `(?=[A-Z0-9.-]*\d)[A-Z0-9]{2,}[-\.][A-Z0-9]{2,}…` is written for uppercase codes and compiled case-insensitively at both call sites, which lets `[A-Z0-9]` match lowercase. The same device capture extracted `behavior.42`, `contexts.54`, `movements.40`, `ed.48`, `min.43` and `D2-autoreceptors` as PartNumber entities — those are superscript reference markers welded to the preceding word. Both sites now wrap the pattern in `(?-i:…)`, which cancels the option locally regardless of how the caller compiles it. The standards regex in `HybridSearchService` had the same defect: `UL` scored inside "Stim**ul**ation" and `EN` inside "**en**dogenous", earning a neuroscience sentence +3 specification points for containing normal English; it is now anchored and case-sensitive. `[evidence_level: device_log_proven + build_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: SpecificationDetector.swift:69, SpecificationExtractor.swift:1294, HybridSearchService standards pattern]`
- **[Retrieval]** **Reference lists are demoted, because nothing in the boilerplate penalty knew what a bibliography was.** A citation page is simultaneously the best keyword match and the worst evidence in a paper. From a shipped answer's citation map, 2 of 20 sources were reference entries (`S16 = …74. Chang CH, Grace AA…`, `S20 = …58. Menegas W…`), and Self-RAG accepted it at 81% confidence with relevance 63%. `RAGEngine.computeBibliographyPenalty` joins `computeTOCPenalty` and `computeQuestionBankPenalty` at **both** existing application sites — the heuristic rerank and the post-cross-encoder pass. It requires two of three orthogonal signals (author-initial runs, journal year/volume/page tails, numbered entries opening on a surname) and scales with density, because any one signal alone has a false positive: methods prose cites authors, results tables carry year figures, procedures open on digits. A penalty rather than a filter, matching its siblings, so a reference list can still win when the question is genuinely about what a paper cited. `nonisolated static` so it is testable without an engine; 6 tests, four of which assert prose is **not** demoted. `[evidence_level: device_log_proven + test_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: PostFixQuery.txt citation map; BibliographyPenaltyTests]`
- **[Orchestration]** **The source-only gate is no longer silent, after three device runs failed to reach it.** `sourceOnlyOutcomeIfNeeded` returned `nil` from four guards with no logging, which is indistinguishable from a stage that ran and found nothing — so three attempts to verify `dbff15a` on device all missed, and the gate was reasoned about from code twice and described wrongly both times. `isExtractiveFirst` is true only for `.lookup` and `.tableLookup`, and the call sites add `(answerIntentIsExtractive || isPrecisionValueQuery(question))` on top. Each guard now names itself, and `classifyAnswerIntent` logs the intent it computed for every query — wrapping the classifier rather than editing its dozen-plus return branches, since instrumenting a subset is how this repository has produced three separate defects. Both under categories that reach a shareable trace. No behaviour change. `[evidence_level: code_verified + build_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: three captures with 0 occurrences of "SourceOnly"]`

- **[Ingestion]** **Device-verified: page text now matches Vision's own transcript order on all eight pages, and the diagnostic probes are removed.** Page 1's stored text reads *"dynamics that regulate diverse aspects of motivation-related / behavior. Dopamine and serotonin transiently modulate / moment-to-moment behavior at timescales ranging from / sub-second to minutes…"* against the previous **2, 1, 4, 3**. `layoutTextChars` is 5,717 in every capture before and after, confirming the text was only ever misordered — nothing lost, nothing read across a gutter. The `TRANSCRIPT PROBE` and `PAGE TEXT PROBE` added in `f879ad9` are removed now that they have done their job; the three comparator unit tests replace them and, unlike a log line, fail against the old predicate. `[evidence_level: device_log_proven + test_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: PostPostFixAgain.txt all 8 pages; 287 tests 0 failures]`
- **[Ingestion]** **Withdrawn: repeated non-English language detection is not a scrambling signal.** `952d85f` and the two-column row both proposed the 118-of-552 non-English detections on a single English paper as a free, high-signal interleaving detector. The corrected capture disproves it — with ordering fixed and every page matching the transcript, the distribution barely moved, 126 non-English chunks to 123. They are reference-list entries such as *"Weissbourd B, Ren J, DeLoach KE, Guenthner CJ, Miyamichi K"*: surnames and initials with no function words, which any language recogniser reads as Indonesian or Dutch. It was detecting bibliographies, not damage. Withdrawn in `Docs/INGESTION_PIPELINE.md` and on the row rather than left as a follow-up nobody could close. `[evidence_level: device_log_proven, confidence: exact, evidence_source: lang distribution across PostFix.txt and PostPostFixAgain.txt]`

- **[Ingestion]** **A page's lines were ordered by a comparator that was not a strict weak ordering, so they came out transposed.** This is the real defect behind the "two-column" damage, and it is neither a column defect nor a Vision defect. In `buildReadingOrderText`, elements were ordered with `if abs(lhs.topY - rhs.topY) > 0.02 { return lhs.topY > rhs.topY }; return lhs.minX < rhs.minX`. Two things are wrong. **The threshold is larger than the quantity it compares**: a journal page carries roughly 55 lines, so consecutive lines sit about 0.015 apart in normalised coordinates, under 0.02, so the vertical test never fired between neighbours and `minX` decided instead — ordering body text by left edge, which is meaningless inside a column and puts an indented or hyphenated line ahead of the line above it. **And it is non-transitive**: lines at 0.500, 0.485 and 0.470 make both inner pairs compare equal while the outer pair compares ordered, and `sorted(by:)` is documented as producing an unspecified result given such a predicate. Replaced by `LayoutAwareExtractor.readingOrderPrecedes(lhsTopY:lhsMinX:rhsTopY:rhsMinX:)`, which sorts strictly by position down the page and uses the left edge only for an exact tie — no epsilon is needed, because `groupBlocksByLine` has already collapsed a physical line into one element using a tolerance derived from real glyph height. Applied to **all three** call sites in that file, not the one that was proven, and the same non-transitive shape in `detectAndSeparateTables` is now expressed as row-bucket quantisation, which keeps its legitimate row-then-left-to-right intent while being transitive. `[evidence_level: device_log_proven + build_verified + test_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: PostFixAgain.txt page 1 TRANSCRIPT PROBE vs PAGE TEXT PROBE; 287 tests 0 failures]`
- **[Ingestion]** **`groupBlocksByLine` matched a block to a line through `Dictionary.keys.first`, which iterates in hash order.** A block within tolerance of more than one line joined an arbitrary one, so grouping depended on Vision's block order and on the per-process hash seed. Blocks are now consumed top-to-bottom and matched to the nearest line from sorted keys. **Recorded honestly: this is a real defect and it was not the cause of the transposition.** It was fixed in `32f340e` on the theory that it was, and the next device capture was byte-identical — `layoutTextChars=5717` before and after, same transposition — which is what redirected the search to the comparator above. `[evidence_level: code_verified + build_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: PostFix.txt vs PostFixAgain.txt, identical output across the change]`

- **[Ingestion]** **The Vision path grouped a page's lines through a dictionary lookup that returns an arbitrary match, so lines came out transposed.** This is the actual two-column defect, and it is not a column defect at all. The paired probes from `f879ad9` settled it in one device run: `DocumentObservation.text.transcript` for page 1 reads *"…dynamics that regulate diverse aspects of motivation-related behavior. Dopamine and serotonin transiently modulate moment-to-moment behavior at timescales ranging from sub-second to minutes…"* — correct and continuous — while the text that actually won for that page (`winner=layoutText` on all 8 pages) emitted those same lines in the order **2, 1, 4, 3**. Adjacent pairs swapped. Pages 3 and 5, which take the PDFKit spatial path and carry its `speci c` ligature drops, read correctly, which localises the defect to `LayoutAwareExtractor`. In `groupBlocksByLine`, `groups.keys.first { abs($0 - block.topY) < lineTolerance }` iterates a `Dictionary` in hash order, so a block within tolerance of more than one line joined an arbitrary one — making the grouping depend on Vision's block order and on the per-process hash seed, so the same page could group differently between runs. Blocks are now consumed top-to-bottom and matched to the *nearest* line from sorted keys. **Nothing was read across the gutter and no text was lost**, which is why this survived a printable-ratio and entropy quality gate: every word was present and correctly recognised, in the wrong order. `[evidence_level: device_log_proven + build_verified + test_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: PostFix.txt TRANSCRIPT PROBE vs PAGE TEXT PROBE, all 8 pages; 284 tests 0 failures]`
- **[Ingestion]** **Correction to this release's own user-facing note.** The entry added in `952d85f` described two-column PDFs as being "read straight across" and welded at the gutter. That is **not** what was happening, and the note is corrected in all three user-facing files rather than deleted. `952d85f` itself stands: `extractTextWithSpatialOrdering` genuinely did merge across a gutter on the PDFKit path and now has 7 tests where it had none, but that path handles 2 of the 8 pages in the affected document and found nothing to correct on either. `[evidence_level: device_log_proven, confidence: exact, evidence_source: identical language-detection distribution before and after 952d85f; horizontal-gap log fired 0 times]`

### Changed
- **[Ingestion]** **Temporary probes recording which function actually emits a page's text, because two fixes have now been aimed at the wrong path.** `952d85f` corrected a real defect in `extractTextWithSpatialOrdering`, but that path handles 2 of 8 pages in the affected capture and found nothing to correct on either; the language-detection distribution after it is byte-identical to before, so the stored text did not change. Both prior diagnoses were inferred from reading code rather than traced to the function that produced the bytes. `TRANSCRIPT PROBE` in `StructuredDocumentParser` records `DocumentObservation.text.transcript` per page — Vision's own linearised reading order, and the prime suspect, since the column-aware `assembleSpatiallyOrderedFallbackText` in that same file is unreachable from the primary path because it sits behind the `RecognizeTextRequest` fallback. `PAGE TEXT PROBE` in `DocumentProcessor` records which candidate won for that page. Both sample from a third of the way in, because mastheads span the full width and read correctly either way, and both escape newlines so one page is one grep-able line. `Log.info` under `.ingestion` so they reach a shareable trace. No behaviour change; to be removed once the defect is located. `[evidence_level: build_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: PostFix.txt — 0 occurrences of the horizontal-gap log, identical lang distribution before and after 952d85f]`

### Fixed
- **[Ingestion]** **Two-column PDFs were read across the gutter, and the signal that would have prevented it was destroyed before the code that looks for it ran.** `extractTextWithSpatialOrdering` grouped words into a `SpatialLine` on a vertical test alone. `page.string` on a two-column PDF emits words row by row across the gutter, so a left-column word and a right-column word at the same height arrive adjacent at the same Y and were merged into one line — whose `xPosition` is the **mean** of its words' midpoints and therefore lands near the page centre. `detectColumnBoundaries` then looks for a gap wider than 10% of the page across those means, finds none because every line sits at the centre, returns empty, and the single-column branch sorts by Y and interleaves the columns. It returns non-nil, so no caller can tell. **Neither the earlier tie-break fix nor a smarter `detectColumnBoundaries` could have addressed this**, which is why that entry carries `symptom_attribution_still_open`: both operate on X positions already averaged across the gutter. `DocumentProcessor.spatialLineBreak(wordBounds:currentLineBounds:currentLineY:)` now returns `.none`/`.verticalGap`/`.horizontalGap` and treats a horizontal discontinuity as a line break — a move back to or past the line's own left edge, or a forward jump wider than `1.5 ×` glyph height floored at 8pt, which is a gutter rather than a word space. Scaling by glyph height keeps it correct at any page size. Lines then stay inside one column, `xPosition` becomes bimodal, and the existing column grouping and per-column assembly work unchanged. Extracted as `nonisolated static` taking only geometry, so this is the first part of the two-column path exercisable without ingesting a journal PDF on a device; **7 new cases** in `DocumentProcessorTests` cover the gutter crossing, the left-margin wrap, ordinary word spacing, the next row, both no-line-started guards and threshold scaling. A page whose words arrive across a gutter now logs it, so single-column and undetected-two-column pages are no longer indistinguishable in a trace. Device evidence: a stored chunk read "…in locomotors are more diverse, consisting of Gi-coupled 5-HT, and 5-HTS. tion, reinforcement learning…", and language detection returned non-English on 118 of 552 chunks of one English paper while the quality gate passed it, because printable ratio and entropy stay healthy when two valid columns are shuffled together. `[evidence_level: code_verified+test_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: DocumentProcessorTests 7 cases, 284 tests 0 failures; device capture 2026-08-24; not yet device-verified]`
- **[UI]** **The ingestion overlay broke "Processing uploads" mid-word.** `Text(headerTitle)` in `IngestionQueueOverlay.headerView` carried no line limit, so when `headerControls` left the label less width than its longest word SwiftUI broke *inside* the word, rendering "Process / ing / uploads" across three lines on a 6.3" device. Both labels in that `VStack` are now `lineLimit(1)` with a minimum scale factor and tail truncation, and the `VStack` takes `layoutPriority(1)` so it claims width ahead of the trailing `Spacer`. Cosmetic, and it is the one piece of chrome visible during every single import. `[evidence_level: build_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: owner screenshot 2026-08-24; IngestionQueueOverlay.swift headerView]`
- **[Orchestration]** **The source-only stage destroyed the answer it was asked to verify.** Observed on device 2026-08-24: the reasoning chain produced a 3,419-character, 472-word answer, Self-RAG accepted it at 95% relevance and 80% confidence, and the final response was **9 words**. No inference ran after the trim — the last `Sustain STOP LLM Inference` precedes it and the following 190 log lines are vector-store reload noise — so those 9 words are a local fallback emitted after the stage was starved of its own input. Two independent causes, both fixed. First, the drop loop's stop condition was `> promptBudget`, the whole budget, so the evidence set was free to consume all of it and `candidateBudget` collapsed to 50 of 3,415 characters. `evidenceBudgetShare` (0.7) existed and was applied to `snippetLimit` and nowhere else, so the intent was documented and never implemented; the loop is now bounded by that share, guaranteeing the candidate its 30%. Second, `snippetLimit` was sized against snippet characters while `renderEvidencePrompt` adds an XML wrapper, the source name, the page and a 36-character chunk UUID per record, plus three domain elements in strict mode — so the drop loop fired on every full-evidence run rather than as an exception. The wrapper is now measured from a probe render rather than guessed, which is the same class of miscalculation the 2026-08-19 note in that file already records once. Note the provenance: this stage was budget-sized deliberately to fix the context overflow observed on 2026-08-18, and that fix is what introduced this. `[evidence_level: device_log_proven + build_verified + test_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: log lines 10964-11258; SourceOnlyAnswerService.swift:276-311]`
- **[Orchestration]** **A verification stage that cannot read its own input now declines instead of overwriting.** `sourceOnlyOutcomeIfNeeded` gated on `supportedClaims.count > 0` and `fidelityScore >= 0.72`, and neither can see how much of the answer the drafter was shown, because both are computed over whatever fragment survived the trim — a confident verdict about 1.5% of an answer is indistinguishable from a confident verdict about all of it. `SourceOnlyAnswerOutcome` now carries `candidateCoverage`, and the caller keeps the generated answer when coverage falls below 0.85. The floor is high deliberately: this stage's output *replaces* the answer, so partial input makes the replacement unsound rather than merely weaker, and the answer it would replace has already passed verification. The comment in that function already named the failure mode — "not when it downgrades a plausible answer into a brittle abstention or ultra-thin rewrite" — so this makes an existing intent enforceable rather than aspirational. Fourth instance of this repository's recurring shape: a stage discards most of its input and reports success. `[evidence_level: code_verified + build_verified + test_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: RAGService.swift sourceOnlyOutcomeIfNeeded; SourceOnlyAnswerService.swift:139-159]`
- **[Indexing]** **The app deleted a just-imported document's vectors itself, and logged nothing when it did.** This is the 0-chunk phantom, and `0350083` was never the cause: the device capture of 2026-08-24 shows the 196 chunks correctly addressed to the library the import started in, despite a library switch mid-ingest at log line 3122. They reached disk at 6675 and again with the RAPTOR L1 summary at 6866, and `WorkspaceSyncService` removed them at 6685 and 6881. `RAGService` persists vectors as soon as embedding finishes and saves the document record later, so for that whole span the library reads as having no documents while its store is being filled. `synchronizeVectorStore` builds `allowedDocumentIds` from that metadata, finds it empty, filters every loaded chunk out as foreign, and the `guard allowedDocumentIds.isEmpty else { ... return }` falls through to `removeVectorStoreArtifacts`. An empty document set was being read as proof the index is garbage; during an import it is proof the metadata has not caught up. **A library switch widens the window and is not required to hit it** — every import is exposed. `synchronizeVectorStore` now refuses to delete when the chunks it just read are non-zero, which is the same rule the branch immediately above it already states in prose: a stale index is recoverable, a deleted one is not. The residual cost is a store left behind by a genuinely empty library after a partial chunk-removal failure, which leaks disk rather than losing work. Genuinely deleted libraries are unaffected; they are cleaned up by the separate `removedContainerIDs` path at `:1391`. `[evidence_level: device_log_proven + build_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: Delete+IngestANDCHANGELIBRARIESMIDINGEST+Query+Rebuild.txt lines 3122, 6673-6951; two independent analysis passes converged on the same mechanism]`
- **[Indexing]** **A vector-store deletion is no longer silent, which is why the defect above took three sessions to find.** `removeVectorStoreArtifacts` was a bare `try?` loop with no logging, while every non-destructive operation in the same file logs, so a device capture of a real vector-store loss contained no evidence of the loss — only two clean `Persisted` lines and, seventy lines later, a debug-level `nothing to merge` that reads as benign. It now takes a mandatory `reason` and reports what it removed and why. The parameter has no default and all eleven call sites are labelled, because this repository has produced three separate defects in three days from applying a change to a subset of its call sites. Logged under `.ingestion` rather than `.vectorDB`: only `LoggingConfiguration.fileLogCategories` reaches the trace a user can share, `.vectorDB` is not in it, and `writeToFile` has no level filter, so adding the category wholesale would put roughly 1,311 `loadVectorChunks` debug lines per session into a 500 KB rotating file and evict the evidence these lines exist to provide. `[evidence_level: code_verified + build_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: WorkspaceSyncService.swift:2756-2783; LoggingConfiguration.swift:219, :401]`

### Added
- **[General]** **`scripts/asc_healthcheck.rb`, because Apple returns one opaque 401 for four different causes.** The App Store Connect metadata push failed for days across two sessions, and both diagnosed it from the error text rather than by testing. `APP_STORE_CONNECT_API_KEY_PATH` pointed at `ApiKey_5UNPFIPXPPRC.p8` — a 12-character id, where Apple issues 10 — while a working Admin key sat unused in `~/Downloads`. `JT97AQ3U4U` was separately revoked, so the "rotation" replaced a dead key with a file that had never been an ASC key at all, and the identical 401 made the two indistinguishable. The check prints the configured key, validates id length, filename and file mode, authenticates against the live API and confirms `Gunndamental.OpenIntelligence` is reachable, in about two seconds, without printing key material, the issuer UUID or the signed token. It catches the actual defect before the network call, on id length alone. `Docs/ai/RUNBOOK.md` previously pointed at `.env.appstore` for credentials, which defines `ASC_KEY_ID`/`ASC_ISSUER_ID` that no lane reads; that section now documents the three `APP_STORE_CONNECT_*` variables the `Fastfile` actually consumes, and the UTF-8 locale `deliver` requires. `[evidence_level: measured, confidence: exact, evidence_source: five keys probed against GET /v1/apps 2026-08-24; both healthcheck paths run]`

- **[UI]** **The 5.0 release notes described 4.9, and the user-facing changelog was missing the largest change in the release.** `fastlane/metadata/en-US/release_notes.txt` still carried the 4.9 text, so the App Store listing for 5.0 advertised the previous release. Rewritten against the verified change set and pushed, 3,996 characters against Apple's 4,000 limit, read back from the API and confirmed byte-for-byte. Separately, the embedding arc — the tokenizer padding defect (`2753d15`), the mean-pooling re-export (`3ea5cd9`) and the pipeline fingerprint (`3b48c88`) — appeared in `CHANGELOG.md` but in none of the three user-facing files, despite being the release's largest measured retrieval change. A `Search` section now covers it in `Docs/USER_CHANGELOG.md`, `OpenIntelligence/Resources/VersionHistory.md` and `WHATS_NEW.md`, including the part that reads badly and is true: a library built before this release keeps its old index and will never be prompted to rebuild, because `embeddingFingerprint` was nil on every existing container and took the silent-adopt branch. `VersionHistory.md` had also drifted from `Docs/USER_CHANGELOG.md` by one bullet, which `VersionHistoryTests` asserts byte-equality on; the two are identical again at 42,425 bytes. `[evidence_level: measured+code_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: ASC appStoreVersionLocalizations read-back; diff of all three files]`
- **[UI]** **Two user-facing claims were stronger than their own roadmap rows.** "Four separate ways a library could lose its ability to answer ... found and closed" claimed four closures where two are device-proven and two — the queue-vetoed rebuild paths — have never been exercised, which the Notion row states outright. Reworded to say what is verified and to name the rest as fixed-not-verified, in all three files. Per `oi-claim-audit` nothing was deleted: the mid-import-switch claim (`0350083`) was checked and kept, because it is accurate about the mechanism it describes even though the same symptom later reproduced from a second cause. Two further candidates were checked and excluded from the App Store text rather than reworded: the two-column PDF entry, whose own tag reads `symptom_attribution_still_open` and which fixed sort determinism rather than column detection, and five orchestration fixes dated 2026-08-03 that were assumed to be 5.0 work — App Store Connect shows iOS 4.9 shipped as build 265 uploaded 17:28 that day, and `acfbfbd` at 17:17 was HEAD, so all five shipped in 4.9. `[evidence_level: code_verified+measured, confidence: exact, evidence_source: Notion rows 3c0/3c1 device captures; ASC build 265 uploadedDate against git author times]`

### Added
- **[Retrieval]** **The debug validation harness now emits rerank-stage chunk text, not only final.** `STAGE SOURCES` has carried chunk ids for every stage since `cb82471`, but only the final stage's chunks carried text — `passage_recall` could therefore ask "did the gold span reach the model" and nothing earlier, so the 10-of-24 cases (`passage-level-1`) where it never arrived could not be told apart: never retrieved, or retrieved and ranked, then cut by the context budget during assembly. Those are different problems with different fixes. `buildReport` now emits a `RERANK CHUNK TEXT` block, same one-line-per-chunk escaped-newline format as the existing `RETRIEVED CHUNK TEXT`, sourced from `traces.first(where: { $0.stage == .rerank })` rather than `response.retrievedChunks` — same `RetrievedChunk` type flows through both, so no new plumbing. Debug-harness only, `#if DEBUG`-gated, not on any production path. `[evidence_level: build_verified+manually_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: BenchmarkRuns/smoke-rerank-text, 1-case smoke run, rerank block captured 90 chunks against 7 at final]`

### Added
- **[UI]** **The app icon now follows the device's dark appearance.** The AppIcon catalog keeps the existing light mark and adds a matching dark luminosity rendition through the universal iOS slot, so Home Screen icon selection can follow the user's device appearance. `[evidence_level: build_verified, confidence: high_pending_device_validation, evidence_source: OpenIntelligence/Resources/Assets/Assets.xcassets/AppIcon.appiconset/Contents.json; xcrun actool; assetutil UIAppearanceDark rendition]`
- **[Ingestion]** **A load-time check that the token counter actually counts.** `DocumentProcessor.verifyTokenizerCounts` encodes a one-word string and a 180-word string when the tokenizer loads and logs an error if they measure the same, which is the signature of an encoder padding to a fixed width. This is the check that would have caught the padding defect on the day it shipped: the logs read `maxTokens=128/430` on 3,910 of 3,910 recorded ingestions, and a constant sitting inside a plausible range looks exactly like a working measurement. Behavioural rather than configuration-based on purpose, so it holds however a future tokenizer expresses padding and needs no parsing of `tokenizer.json`. It logs rather than traps, because a wrong token count degrades retrieval silently without corrupting data, and refusing to ingest would be the worse failure.
- **[General]** **`--seed` on the quality matrix, because a temperature comparison cannot be run under greedy sampling.** Greedy always takes the highest-probability token and therefore ignores temperature entirely, so a 0.7 against 0.3 run under greedy would emit identical output in both arms and return a null result that means nothing. A controlled temperature comparison needs random sampling with the draws held fixed, which is what a seed provides: same draws, different distribution. `InferenceConfig.seed` exists and is already threaded to Apple's `GenerationOptions`, but defaults to nil and nothing set it.
- **[General]** **`--sampling` and `--temperature` on the quality matrix, because two runs of the same build were not comparable to each other.** `InferenceConfig.samplingStrategy` defaults to `.topK` with `seed` nil, so every run drew a different sample. Measured consequence: one case returned 3613, then 68, then 3357 characters across three runs whose code differences cannot account for the swing, which is noise larger than most effects worth measuring and makes an A/B unreadable rather than merely imprecise. The override is applied in `LLMService` at the single point an `InferenceConfig` becomes `GenerationOptions`, not at the dozen construction sites, because a rule applied to a subset of call sites is not applied and this repository has made that mistake three times in two days. Both keys are cleared when their flag is absent so a stale value cannot silently change a later run, and both are recorded in `run_config.json` so a resume under different sampling is refused rather than merged. The two flags are separate on purpose: `--sampling greedy` answers "can I compare two runs", `--temperature` answers "is a lower temperature better for grounded answers", and the shipped RAG preset uses 0.7 labelled "balanced creativity" for an app whose product promise is answers attributable to the user's own documents.
- **[General]** **`scripts/watch_benchmark_defects.sh`, which watches a benchmark run for failure shapes instead of for completion.** The previous watcher reported terminal states only, so on 2026-08-15 a defect visible in case 1 at minute 4, the on-device model echoing the prompt's own `[SEARCH: query]` placeholder, went unread until three cases had burned, and the correction cycle was a full run per fix. Reporting per case against a six-case run cut that to about twenty five minutes and surfaced four separate defects the same afternoon, including a guard that had been applied to only one of two call sites. It prints a line only for a case carrying a known signature, a heartbeat every ten clean cases, and every terminal state including a crash or a stall, because a filter matching only the happy path is silent through a crash and silence then looks identical to progress. Each signature traces to a defect it actually caught. Validated in both directions before committing: it flags the placeholder defect on case 1 of the 2026-08-15 run and stays quiet on the healthy cases of the current one.
- **[General]** **`--rag-validation-questions`, because the suggested-question path had no headless coverage of any kind.** The harness only ever ran queries, so the chips offered before the user types anything were changed on 2026-08-15 and verified by compiling. The framing that this needed "a device" was wrong: Apple Foundation Models runs on the development Mac exactly as it does on a phone, and the whole benchmark has been doing real on-device inference all along. What was missing was a way to invoke the path, not different hardware. First run against a residential lease, a document type QASPER cannot represent, returned six questions with six distinct openings and none of the research-paper framing the prompt used to force. Two of the six ask "why" about rules the document states without justifying, which is the next thing to improve. `[evidence_level: measured, confidence: high, evidence_source: headless probe run against /private/tmp/oi-probe/Residential-Lease-Agreement.md]`
- **[Orchestration]** **The verification loop now records the answer it discards.** `runVerificationLoop` overwrites `currentAnswer` with whatever recursive research returns, and the replaced text was written nowhere, so the 2026-08-15 run showing 18 answers collapse from a median 3510 characters to 181 could not settle whether the discarded answer was the better one. Only the survivor was ever recorded. Both lengths and both citation counts are now logged at each replacement site. Logging only, with no behaviour change, because 12 of those 18 discarded answers had already failed citation grounding and "longer is better" is not established. The citation-index fix in this release changes the grounding scores that feed the gate, so the next run measures the corrected system rather than the old one.
- **[General]** **`scripts/sweep_fusion_weight.py`, which answers a fusion-weight question from a finished run instead of from a new one.** A run costs about 4.7 hours and the whole machine, and the hybrid fusion weight is a parameter of an arithmetic step that executes after retrieval, so paying a pipeline execution per value was paying for the wrong thing. `RAGEngine.reciprocalRankFusion` is a pure function of the two arms' rank orders, the weight and `k = 60`, so the script replays it across the whole range and prints the curve in seconds.
