- **[General]** **Swept all 144 Notion roadmap rows against the code**, after an earlier pass had verified only about eight of them. Two rows had been sitting in To Do since 2026-04-07 while the features shipped underneath them: *iCloud Sync for Document Library* (delivered by `WorkspaceSyncService`, whose cross-device library reconciliation is most of what 4.9 hardened) and *Conversation History & Sessions* (shipped as Evidence Threads, which carry titles, persist through `EvidenceThreadStore.saveThread`, and have their own sidebar and App Intents). Both closed and backdated. Two completed rows were annotated where their numbers no longer describe the app: *8 Agentic @Tool Functions* (four are registered now) and *Verification Gates A-G (7-Stage)* (the enum runs A through I, with H and I added later and both since corrected in 4.9). *Camera Vision Overlay* stays open but is recorded as near-complete, since the view and its camera stack exist and only the `ChatScreen.swift:547` call site is commented out. `Docs/ROADMAP.md` was the stale side on execution-receipt fidelity: it still listed F-06/F-07 as an open owner decision, when both closed on 2026-07-29 and shipped in 4.8. `[evidence_level: code_verified, confidence: high]`
- **[General]** **Five pipeline stages run in the shipped app and had never been documented; one documented stage does not run at all.** Found by reading `// Step` markers out of `RAGService.swift` rather than by reading a specification, after a walkthrough sourced from the docs asserted behaviour the code does not have. Undocumented and running: `1.5b` Per-Container Vocabulary Expansion, `1.5c` Gazetteer Domain Vocabulary Enrichment, `3.5` Section Metadata Boost, `4.6b` Cross-Reference Resolution, and `4.8` Targeted Spec Retrieval. Documented and **not** running: `5.10` Extractive QA, commented out at `RAGService.swift:12022-12026` with the reason recorded in source, heuristic extraction produced false positives, returning `"three-quarters"` for a fuel-tank-capacity query, and bypassed the LLM entirely when it fired. The stale `Current Implementation Notes` bullet claiming a live "deterministic high-precision lookup override path" is corrected rather than deleted, with the distinction that survives it: `GroundedAnswerPolicy.deterministicExtraction` and the planner's `.deterministic` target are both still real, but `.deterministic` is grouped with `.onDevice` at every execution branch, so it selects a **local route** and not a generation bypass. Two of these matter beyond bookkeeping. `3.5` is the stage `RetrievalStageEvaluator` reports as `boosted`, so the retrieval benchmark has been measuring a stage no specification contained. And `4.8` exists because the cross-encoder carries a measured prose bias, ~0.78 for prose against ~0.30 for tables, which systematically demotes exactly the content that answers specification, dosage, statute-number and financial lookups, it routes around a known reranker weakness rather than retuning it, and both remaining `exact_value` benchmark failures sit in that area. **No user-facing figure was changed:** steps 1–9 carry 28 labels in code against the 23 specified, so `6 + 28 = 34` enumerated and `6 + 27 = 33` active, which makes the public "29-step" figure an undercount rather than an overclaim. Re-baselining a marketing number is an owner decision and the arithmetic is now recorded where that decision can be made against it. `[evidence_level: code_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: RAGService.swift step markers across the query path, :2331-2337, :12022-12040, :12412, GroundedAnswerPolicy.swift:38-66, ModelExecutionPlanner.swift:69-72, FoundationModelRoutePolicy.swift:36]`
- **[General]** **The documented SQLite schema was missing four of its nine tables.** `Docs/Research/RESEARCH_PAPERS_REFERENCE_SHEET.md` Part 10 listed five. `SQLiteFullTextService.swift` creates nine. Added: `chunk_structured` (structured elements kept alongside a chunk rather than flattened into its text), `chunk_table_rows` (table rows stored individually, which is the storage half of the atomic-table guarantee that §9/TableRAG describes only through parsing and chunking), `semantic_query_cache` (previously embedded queries, which is what lets Step 2 be skipped on an exact cache hit), and `documents_vocab` (the `fts5vocab` table that Steps 1.5/1.5b draw on). The `chunks` entry now also records its nine-column shape and which three columns are actually searchable, because the column count is load-bearing for BM25 weight vectors and has already caused one off-by-one that dropped `section_path` out of ranking entirely. `[evidence_level: code_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: SQLiteFullTextService.swift:191-345, :2170]`
- **[General]** **Wrote down how the pipeline's "29 steps" are counted, and retracted the finding that said the figure was wrong.** The number was never wrong; the counting rule was simply never recorded, and its absence has now produced two independent miscounts a month and a half apart. 23 is Steps 1 through 9 inclusive, the query-to-response transformation proper. Step 0 warms a container vocabulary cache before any query exists and Step 10 renders an answer that is already final, so neither is part of that transformation and neither is counted, which is why the enumerated list holds 25 entries while the figure reads 23 and `6 + 23 = 29` holds. `RAG_TECHNICAL.md` now states the rule beside the heading with a shell one-liner that reproduces both numbers, and Part 9 of `RESEARCH_PAPERS_REFERENCE_SHEET.md` carries the same note. **F-3 in that file is retracted**, joining F-1 and F-2, which leaves the 2026-07-31 pipeline-order review with no surviving finding, recorded as such rather than left as three struck items. F-3 had claimed the figure was unenumerated when Part 9 of the very document it was written into had enumerated it a month earlier (`0bf7c9c` 2026-06-30 against `a11da04` 2026-07-31), and had inferred an error from 25-against-23 without asking what the 23 selected. A 2026-08-10 review repeated the identical inference and concluded the total was 31. Both are recorded in the retraction because the reasoning error is the reusable part: when a stated count disagrees with a list, the count may be selecting from the list rather than summing it. No user-facing figure changed, on the website or anywhere else; all of them were already correct. `[evidence_level: code_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: RAG_TECHNICAL.md enumerated list, git history showing label 23 and enumeration 25 unchanged together since 4e24bbb 2026-05-12, git log -S dating Part 9 and F-3]`
- **[General]** Reconciled the documentation set and the Notion roadmap against the shipped 4.9 tree. `Docs/ROADMAP.md` was headed v4.7 (iOS) / v3.0 (macOS) and still listed the 4.7 submission as the open gate two releases after it closed; both platforms now share one version number. `WHATS_NEW.md` stopped at 4.6 while the in-app sheet was keyed 4.9, so 4.7–4.9 are added from `Docs/USER_CHANGELOG.md` and `WhatsNewStore.swift`. `Docs/RepoOS/04_RELEASE_READINESS_DASHBOARD.md` described the PCC entitlement as intentionally absent and gave a grep expecting no match, which would now fail because the entitlement is present and true. The canonical source of truth still listed native PCC execution as unverified, contradicting the owner confirmation recorded in the roadmap. `Docs/BILLING_AND_LIMITS.md` documented the Document Pack as a live product when it is withdrawn from sale and retained only for existing owners. Pipeline and evaluation docs carry explicit "verified at vX, not re-verified since" headers naming the 4.9 changes they do not yet describe, rather than being restamped as current. `[evidence_level: code_verified, confidence: exact]`

## 4.9 - 2026-08-02

<!-- iOS 4.8 was developer-rejected and never shipped, so the iOS 4.9 binary
     carries everything under 4.8 below as well. macOS 4.8 was approved, so for
     Mac users only this section is new. -->

### Changed
- **[UI]** Keyed the What's New sheet to 4.9 and gave it the iCloud item. `WhatsNewStore.releases` is looked up by `CFBundleShortVersionString`, so a sheet still keyed `"4.8"` would never open on a 4.9 build. One list serves both platforms: iPhone and iPad arrive from 4.7 and find every item new, while Mac arrives from the already-released 4.8 and finds only the iCloud item new, the alternative was a per-platform key for a single bullet. Also fixes `WhatsNewView`'s `#Preview`, which force-unwrapped `releases["4.8"]` and would have crashed the moment the key moved. `[evidence_level: build_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: WhatsNewStore.swift, WhatsNewView.swift]`
- **[Infrastructure]** Cut 4.9 for both platforms. `MARKETING_VERSION` moves 4.8 to 4.9 across all six app and extension targets; `ci_post_clone.sh` overwrites it from the first numbered CHANGELOG heading on every Xcode Cloud build, so this only keeps local builds honest about what ships. The 4.9 heading sits above 4.8 rather than renaming it, which preserves macOS 4.8's shipped history while still giving CI 4.9 to stamp. `[evidence_level: build_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: project.pbxproj, ci_scripts/ci_post_clone.sh]`

### Fixed
- **[Orchestration]** **The planner no longer abstains on strong evidence because it read the wrong chunk's score.** `makePostRetrievalModelPlan` derived its top relevance score from `chunks.first?.similarityScore`. By the time the planner sees that array it has been through MMR, cross-reference expansion, the spec sniper, corrective lexical/page retrieval, which appends chunks carrying no vector score at all, and finally Lost-in-the-Middle position reordering, which rearranges purely for attention placement. Slot zero is not the best chunk and has no reason to be. Device log 2026-08-03: a query whose confidence gate reported `top 1.16` across 21 chunks and 1002 words of context, including a fully parsed table, was handed to the planner as `top score 0.01`. `PostRetrievalEvidence.isSufficient` requires `topScore >= 0.20`, so it abstained, the reliability fallback engaged, and the user was shown raw excerpts instead of an answer. Now takes the maximum over the set rather than its head. **This is the root cause of the abstentions instrumented earlier in this release**, the abstention was genuine, but it was triggered by a number that never described the evidence. `[evidence_level: device_log_verified+build_verified+test_verified, confidence: high, evidence_source: RAGService.swift, iPhone A18 Pro log 2026-08-03]`
- **[Retrieval]** **A query rewrite that finds nothing no longer means your documents don't cover it.** Deep Think expands a question into search variations before retrieving. When those variations returned zero chunks, the agentic loop's relevance gate scored 0% and hard-exited with "Retrieved content is irrelevant" after 2 of 8 sessions, reporting a failed paraphrase as a statement about the library. Device log 2026-08-03: "Can you steam these scopes?" was rewritten to "steam scopes", fused 0 chunks, and the user was told their document didn't mention it, while a tool call in the same session searched the same corpus and returned **45 chunks** on steam sterilisation. The rewrite lost the question; the document was never the problem. `executeReasoningChain` now retries with the user's original wording before any downstream gate is allowed to judge relevance, and only stands by the hard exit when that also comes back empty. The retry is skipped when the original query is already among the generated variations, so the common case costs nothing. `[evidence_level: device_log_verified+build_verified+test_verified, confidence: high, evidence_source: AgenticOrchestrator.swift, iPhone A18 Pro log 2026-08-03]`
- **[UI]** **A library that has lost its search index now says so and offers to rebuild it.** Declining an ingestion prompt, discard, stop, pause, or cancel, adds the library to a persisted `selfHealingSuppressedContainers` set, and `enqueueSelfHealingRebuild` then returned silently for that library forever. The library kept its documents, kept working keyword search, and permanently lost semantic retrieval with nothing surfaced. Because the disappearing-documents race manufactured phantom "resume interrupted import?" prompts, every correct dismissal of a bogus prompt quietly disarmed the repair for that library. Device evidence 2026-08-03: Library 3 held 239 chunks indexed in FTS5 with no vector store, and Deep Think answered "I couldn't find information about that in your documents" for a topic the same session's tool call found 45 chunks of. The suppressed branch now warns rather than logging at `.info`, publishes `librariesNeedingIndexRebuild`, and the library shows a rebuild banner. `rebuildSemanticIndex(for:)` clears the suppression before rebuilding, because asking for a repair revokes whatever earlier dismissal disarmed it, otherwise the library falls back into the same silent state the next time its vectors go missing. Rebuilding regenerates embeddings from chunks already on disk: no re-import, no re-OCR, no recreating the library. `[evidence_level: device_log_verified+build_verified+test_verified, confidence: high_for_the_surfacing_partial_for_detection, evidence_source: RAGService.swift, DocumentLibraryView.swift, iPhone A18 Pro log 2026-08-03]`

  **[UI]** Second follow-up, and a correction to the entry above. The suppression account was wrong for this device: `enqueueSelfHealingRebuild` *was* called and the rebuild *did* complete, per the 2026-08-03 log, `Safety guard triggered: vector store is empty but container 336CCAE1 has 1 documents`, then `Queuing single-flight self-healing rebuild`, then `Self-healing rebuild completed successfully`. Self-healing was not disarmed here. The suppression path is real code and can still strand a library, but it was not the cause on this device. The banner is now driven from that safety guard at `RAGService`'s FTS5-rebuild check, which is the detector that reliably fires and which already existed before any of this work, it was simply never wired to anything user-visible. The flag clears when a rebuild completes, so a library that repairs itself shows the banner briefly and one that cannot keeps it. `[evidence_level: device_log_verified+build_verified+test_verified, confidence: high, evidence_source: RAGService.swift, iPhone A18 Pro log 2026-08-03]`

  **[UI]** First follow-up: the initial version hung the surfacing off `enqueueSelfHealingRebuild`, which only runs when ingestion-time detection fires. Device evidence showed that path was never called once across a full session while `WorkspaceSyncService` logged "has 1 document(s) and no vector store yet" repeatedly, one service knew, another drove the banner, and nothing connected them, so no banner appeared. `evaluateSemanticIndexHealth(for:)` now runs on container switch, where the state is cheap to read and the user is about to ask something. It compares the library's documents against what the vector store itself reports via `count()`, not the container's cached `totalChunks`, the cached figure is what claimed 420 chunks while the store held none, because it counts what was ingested rather than what is searchable. A read failure returns without flagging: treating a failed read as proof of emptiness is the mistake that made the sync delete a working index earlier in this release, and it is not repeated here. `[evidence_level: device_log_verified+build_verified+test_verified, confidence: high, evidence_source: RAGService.swift, iPhone A18 Pro log 2026-08-03]`
- **[Retrieval]** **Answers synthesised across several chunks are no longer rejected for it.** Gate E measured query-term coverage per chunk and took the maximum, which asks whether any *single* chunk contains 55% of the query's anchor terms. That rejects precisely the answers retrieval exists to produce: combining evidence across chunks is the point, and requiring one chunk to carry the whole query penalises correct multi-chunk answers. Device log 2026-08-03 carries both numbers, `[LexicalRelevance] 3/5 keywords found = 60%` across the evidence against `Gate E: query coverage 40% < 55%` for the best single chunk, because "cleaning" and "deposits" landed in different chunks of the same document. The generated answer was discarded and the user was shown raw chunk text. Coverage is now the better of combined-evidence and best-single-chunk, so a chunk that does carry the query passes exactly as before, and the failure detail prints both figures so the distinction is never ambiguous again. `evaluateTopicalAlignment` already built `combinedEvidence` and used it for the strong-anchor and section-term checks; coverage simply never used it. `[evidence_level: device_log_verified+build_verified+test_verified, confidence: high, evidence_source: VerificationGateService.swift, iPhone A18 Pro log 2026-08-03]`

  Correction to the entry below: domain isolation was initially identified as the cause of that discarded answer. It was not. `.domainIsolation` is absent from the `rejectingGates` set in `RAGService`, so Gate I failing never routes to the extractive fallback. Gate E did. The Gate I fix is a genuine false-positive repair that affects `SourceOnlyAnswerService`, but it does not change this symptom.
- **[Retrieval]** **Domain isolation no longer blocks answers drawn from a single document.** Gate I abstained on a laparoscope cleaning query with "mixed evidence across incompatible domains: IN VITRO vs IN VIVO" while all three retrieved chunks were `[S1/S2/S3: Autoclavable Laparoscopes IFU.pdf]`. A surgical IFU uses both phrases in one author's voice; there were no sources to mix. The generated answer was discarded and the user was shown raw chunk text instead. `DomainIsolationService` now counts distinct `documentId`s among the *retrieved chunks*, not the library's document count, and relaxes only the cross-domain-mixing clause when that count is one. Query mismatch, top-chunk mismatch, and coverage are relevance judgements and remain in force. Evidence spanning two or more documents still blocks exactly as before, so genuine cross-document contamination in a multi-document library is unaffected. `[evidence_level: device_log_verified+build_verified, confidence: high, evidence_source: DomainIsolationService.swift, iPhone A18 Pro log 2026-08-03]`
- **[Orchestration]** **A planner abstention no longer reports itself as the model being unavailable.** `RAGService` threw `modelNotAvailable` when `ModelExecutionTarget` resolved to `.abstain`, so the log and UI both claimed the selected model was gone. Device log 2026-08-03: that fired at Step 6, and on-device generation completed 32 words 4 seconds later, the model was healthy throughout. This string has now sent two separate debugging sessions after a working model, including the Deep Think investigation in 4.8. Adds `RAGServiceError.routingAbstained` with copy that does not blame the model, and logs the chunk count and top similarity score the planner actually saw. **This does not fix the abstention itself**, with `sourceChunks` threaded correctly and a top score of 1.14 against a 0.20 sufficiency floor, the cause is still unidentified. The change stops the misattribution and instruments the real inputs so the next occurrence is diagnosable. `[evidence_level: device_log_verified+build_verified, confidence: high_for_the_misreport_unresolved_for_the_abstention, evidence_source: RAGService.swift, ChatScreen.swift]`
- **[UI]** **The extractive fallback no longer prints chunk-boundary artefacts.** When generation is unavailable the fallback lists retrieved chunks verbatim, so a chunk that begins partway through a sentence is shown that way. Device log produced a bullet reading ", Inspection, and Testing", a chunk starting inside "Maintenance, Inspection, and Testing", and another beginning "d Laparoscopes", a word cut in half. `tidiedExcerpt` strips leading punctuation and drops a severed opening token of one or two lowercase characters, and an excerpt that reduces to nothing is omitted rather than rendered as an empty bullet. This treats the symptom: the underlying defect is that a chunk should not start mid-word, which is tracked in `Docs/Engineering/RETRIEVAL_UPGRADE_PLAN_2026-08.md`. `[evidence_level: device_log_verified+build_verified, confidence: high_for_the_display_symptom_only, evidence_source: RAGService.swift]`
- **[Infrastructure]** **Documents no longer disappear when an import finishes while a sync pass is running.** This was the actual cause of vanishing documents, including the sample set, and it is not an iCloud fault, it reproduces on one device. `synchronizeConfiguredLibraries` snapshots `documents_metadata.json` at the start of a pass, then merges containers, resolves aliases, and awaits iCloud downloads in `ensureSourcedDocumentsAvailableLocally` before writing its computed set back over that same file. An ingestion completing inside that window writes its own copy, and the pass then overwrites it with a set computed before that document existed. The document is left wholly intact on disk, chunks, embeddings, FTS5 rows, Spotlight entry, with no metadata row pointing at it, so it vanishes from every library at once; and because the ingestion *completed*, no queue item survives to offer a resume, which is why the usual "resume interrupted import?" prompt is absent. Device evidence: `Persisted 240 chunks` then `Saved 2 documents metadata`, then after one pass `Loaded 1 documents (181 chunks)`, with the 240-chunk store still loading correctly throughout. The reason this hid for so long is that the code looks right, both writers already use `NSFileCoordinator`, which makes an individual read or write atomic but does nothing for a read-modify-write assembled from two separately coordinated calls. Fixes: add `coordinatedMergeData(at:transform:)`, which holds a single `NSFileCoordinator` write claim with `.forMerging` across the read and the write so nothing can land between them; convert the sync's local document write to fold in documents that arrived mid-pass rather than overwrite them; and convert `RAGService.saveDocumentsToDisk` the same way, since it blind-writes an in-memory snapshot and is the mirror of the same race, sync adopts another device's document, the local save then erases it. The shared-root document write gets the same treatment for the cross-device case. `[evidence_level: device_log_verified+build_verified+test_verified, confidence: high_for_mechanism_pending_two_device_validation, evidence_source: WorkspaceSyncService.swift, RAGService.swift, iPhone A18 Pro log 2026-08-02]`

  **[Infrastructure]** Libraries are covered by the same guarantee. `containers.json` was initially left as a plain write because `enforceLibraryLimit` throws rather than truncating, and it ran against the computed set only, folding a library in after that check could carry a workspace past its tier limit. Resolved by enforcing against the *merged* set inside the accessor: if the fold would exceed the limit, the write is skipped entirely, which leaves the newly created library intact on disk for the next pass to adopt through the normal path. No library is ever deleted to satisfy a quota. The fold is also alias-aware, because a container id missing from the computed set can still be a pre-merge source for one that is present, and matching on id alone would resurrect a duplicate the merge had just collapsed. The shared-root container write gets the same merge for the cross-device case, without the tier check, the limit is a per-workspace rule enforced on the local write, and refusing to record a library that already exists in iCloud would only hide it from this device. All five workspace metadata writes are now atomic read-modify-writes. `[evidence_level: build_verified+test_verified, confidence: high, evidence_source: WorkspaceSyncService.swift]`
- **[Infrastructure]** **iCloud sync no longer deletes another device's search index.** A library ingested on one device required full re-processing on another, and the second device was deleting the first device's embeddings out of iCloud to get there. `ensureItemsAvailableLocally` guarded on `fileExists` before acting, which skipped exactly the files it existed to download: an iCloud item whose contents have not arrived is represented on disk by a `.<name>.icloud` placeholder, so `fileExists` at the item's own path returns false and `startDownloadingUbiquitousItem` was never called. `vectorStoreExists` used the same check, so the shared store read as absent, `sharedChunks` stayed empty, the merge in `synchronizeVectorStore` resolved to zero chunks, and the empty-resolution branch deleted the shared vector store, a deletion that then propagated back to the device holding the real index. Downstream, `documents_metadata.json` still listed the documents while the chunk count was zero, which is what produced the phantom "resume interrupted import?" prompt. Fixes: detect placeholders and actually request the download, returning the URLs that could not be materialized instead of failing silently; stop bailing when `isUbiquitousItem` is false for an evicted item, and keep polling rather than throwing on the `resourceValues` call that always fails while a file is still a placeholder; fail closed via `WorkspaceSyncError.sharedVectorStoreUnavailable` when a shared store exists but did not download; and never delete an index for a library that still has documents, because a stale index is recoverable and a deleted one is not. Also removes the `fileExists`-only definition of existence from `vectorStoreExists`, which was answering "are the bytes here" for a question about whether data exists. `[evidence_level: code_verified+build_verified+test_verified, confidence: high_pending_two_device_validation, evidence_source: WorkspaceSyncService.swift]`
- **[Infrastructure]** Separated the three causes of an empty vector-store resolution in the sync log. Device logs showed the new deletion guard firing eight times for one container with no detail attached. `mergeVectorChunks` keeps a chunk only when `allowedDocumentIds` contains its `documentId`, so a zero resolution can mean no store exists yet (benign, ingestion has not run), a store exists but read as empty (a failed read), or chunks loaded whose document ids are absent from `documents_metadata.json` (an orphaned index). Only the third is a defect and the log could not distinguish it. Each case now reports separately, the orphaned case prints the mismatched document ids from both sides, and the benign case drops to debug so release builds stop reporting a normal state as an error. The stores stay protected in all three; this is instrumentation, not a root-cause fix. Also confirms the placeholder handling above was not what fired on that device, the FileProvider entries in the same log read `ul:uploading dl:current`, so those files were local and uploading rather than evicted. `[evidence_level: device_log_verified+build_verified+test_verified, confidence: high_for_the_classification_root_cause_still_open, evidence_source: iPhone A18 Pro device log 2026-08-02, WorkspaceSyncService.swift]`
## 4.8 - 2026-07-29

### Fixed
- **[Chunking]** Implemented the macOS document picker. `DocumentPicker` was gated behind `#if canImport(UIKit)`, so the native macOS build fell through to a stub reading "Document picker is unavailable on this platform", Mac users had no way to import documents through it at all. Replaced with an `NSOpenPanel` implementation mirroring the iOS path: the same accepted types (PDF, Office, iWork, text, code, images, audio, video), multiple selection, and the same copy into the managed workspace with a refreshed modification date so the sync sweep does not treat fresh imports as stale. Adds security-scoped resource access, which iOS did not need because its picker hands back copies. `[evidence_level: build_verified, confidence: high, evidence_source: DocumentPicker.swift]`
- **[Orchestration]** Closed a session race in `AppleFoundationLLMService`. `ensureSession` now returns the session it created alongside the route, and both call sites use that value rather than re-reading the shared `session` property. `generate` is `@MainActor async`, so calls interleave at `await` points; during multi-session reasoning one call's session reset could land between another call's `ensureSession` and its read of the property. **This is not the Deep Think fix**, that root cause is the planner-evidence entry below, and this race was never the cause. `[evidence_level: build_verified, confidence: high_for_the_race_not_a_fix_for_deep_think, evidence_source: LLMService.swift]`
- **[Orchestration]** **Deep Think's reasoning chain now reasons.** Root cause: `generateWithProperConsent` defaults `sourceChunks` to `[]`, and 13 of its 14 call sites rely on that default because they pass evidence as an already-rendered `context` string. `executeReasoningChain` is stricter still, it passes `context: ""` deliberately, since `buildChainPrompt` embeds the documents in the prompt text and passing them again double-rendered into 4521-token overflows on a 4096 budget. So `makePostRetrievalModelPlan` computed `chunkCount == 0`, `PostRetrievalEvidence.isSufficient` returned false, the planner set `.abstain`, and `.abstain` threw `RAGServiceError.modelNotAvailable`, while thousands of characters of on-target evidence sat in the prompt. Only Deep Think and Maximum route through that path, which is why Standard was unaffected. The earlier "macOS-specific" diagnosis was wrong: iPhone device logs show the identical failure, so this was cross-platform all along and the prior Known Issue is superseded. Fixes: thread real chunks into the planner (planning-only, never rendered, so no token cost and no re-doubling); pin chain sessions on-device via a new `forceOnDevice` flag, since their prompts are budgeted for 4096 with tools disabled and the now-sufficient evidence would otherwise route eight local passes to PCC; replace `.abstain`'s availability error with a grounded abstention. `[evidence_level: device_verified, confidence: high_for_iOS_unverified_for_macOS, evidence_source: four iPhone A18 Pro device runs 2026-07-30, AgenticOrchestrator.swift, RAGService.swift]`
- **[Orchestration]** Stopped the reasoning chain corrupting its own prompt. `PRIOR FINDINGS` was assembled with raw `String.prefix()` in two places, the condensing path (`prefix(250)` per insight, `prefix(800)` on the join, firing at `chainInsights.count > 3`, i.e. session 5) and `buildChainPrompt` (`prefix(400)`, `prefix(1200)`). Both ended the block mid-word, and a model handed a prompt that stops mid-thought completed the *prompt* rather than answering it, echoing `SESSION OBJECTIVE:` and `ADDITIONAL DOCUMENTS:` back as its insight. Now cut at sentence boundaries. `[evidence_level: device_verified, confidence: high, evidence_source: device runs 2026-07-30]`
- **[Orchestration]** **Maximum can cite its sources again.** Every Maximum device run reported `citations=0/0`, and Self-RAG scored the answers 32–50% relevance because it had nothing to verify against. Cause: `extractFactsFromInsight` keeps whole sentences but truncates each to 150 characters (200 for conceptual queries), and a `[S#]` marker almost always sits at the *end* of its sentence, so the truncation silently ate the attribution. A second pass compresses `content` to 100 characters when the bank exceeds its budget, destroying anything that survived the first. The markers were never deliberately stripped; they were collateral damage from two truncations. `ScoredFact` now carries a dedicated `sources: [String]` field, captured from the **full** sentence before truncation and preserved through compression, and `asContext()` emits it alongside each claim. Only with attribution actually present is the synthesis prompt given a citation instruction, adding one earlier would have induced fabricated markers for claims whose sources were already gone. Deep Think was never affected: it passes untruncated insight text straight through and verifies 13–17 citations. `[evidence_level: build_verified+test_verified, confidence: high, evidence_source: AgenticOrchestrator.swift FactBank, Maximum device traces 2026-07-31]`
- **[Orchestration]** Maximum's final synthesis sees more than three sessions. It received `allInsights.suffix(3).map { $0.prefix(300) }` alongside the ~1500-char FactBank, so on a 50-session run **47 sessions reached the synthesizer only as compressed summary**. A device trace produced a 236-character answer from 22,962 generated tokens, and a caveat the model had correctly identified in session 4, that "serotosocial" in the source is likely an OCR error for "serotonergic", was absent from the final answer, because session 4 was not among the last three. The window now scales with session count (`min(8, max(3, count / 4))`) with the per-insight budget tapering as the count grows, so the block stays bounded. The prompt also now asks explicitly for uncertainty in the findings to be preserved rather than flattened into assertion. `[evidence_level: build_verified+test_verified, confidence: high, evidence_source: Maximum device trace 2026-07-31]`
- **[Orchestration]** User-facing help copy no longer becomes a reasoning finding. `cleanupFinalAnswer` runs on every intermediate session insight, and it contains a NOT-FOUND handler that *replaces* the text whenever it matches `COULDN'T FIND`, `COULD NOT FIND`, `DOCUMENTS DO NOT CONTAIN` and similar. A device run hit this at session 12: the model honestly reported it could not find something in the documents, a correct observation the chain needs, and the whole insight was swapped for *"I searched through your documents but couldn't find specific information about … This information may be in an image, diagram, or table that wasn't extracted as text. You could try: • Checking the original document directly…"*. That help text then entered the fact bank as a finding. The image/diagram explanation is boilerplate, not a diagnosis; the owner noted the corpus is text-indexed in SQLite, so the stated cause was simply wrong. Same class as the abstention text becoming an insight, fixed earlier in `665da0a`. `cleanupFinalAnswer` now takes `isFinalAnswer` (defaulting true, so final answers are unchanged) and the three intermediate call sites, the unlimited loop's per-session insight, the running answer, and folded chain insights, opt out. `[evidence_level: device_verified_for_the_defect+build_verified+test_verified, confidence: high, evidence_source: device session 12 observation 2026-07-31, AgenticOrchestrator.swift:7181]`
- **[Orchestration]** The final synthesis prompt is budgeted against the context window. A device trace showed `Generation: ~3506 tokens` requesting `maxTokens=1500` against a 4096-token on-device window, **5006 into 4096**, so the model was left almost no room and emitted **five tokens**, a 48-character answer (`**5-H11B receptors** controls impulse responses.`) after 22,292 tokens and 16.4 minutes of reasoning. The fact bank was healthy at the time: 11 claims, 5 of 5 sub-questions answered. Nothing was wrong with the evidence; the prompt simply did not fit. This was made worse by the previous change: widening the supplementary block from `suffix(3) × 300` to `suffix(8) × 220` was intended to fix an earlier 236-character answer and instead pushed the prompt further past the window. The supplementary block is now sized from `contextSize − output reserve − service overhead − fact bank − instructions`, trimmed at a sentence boundary, and dropped entirely with a log line when nothing is left. Budgeted against the **on-device** window even when synthesis may escalate to PCC: under-using a 32K window costs nothing, over-filling a 4096 one costs the whole answer, and an On-Device selection makes the small window mandatory. Recomputed: 2396 input tokens available, ~2395 used, plus 1500 output = 3895 against 4096. `[evidence_level: measured+build_verified+test_verified, confidence: high_for_the_arithmetic_unverified_on_device, evidence_source: Maximum device trace 2026-07-31]`
- **[Orchestration]** The claim bank no longer spends context on internal scoring metadata. `FactBank.asContext()` emitted `• claim=<text> [S2]; kind=<evidenceKind>; terms=<top five keywords>` for every claim, and `evidenceKind` and `supportTerms` have exactly one consumer, that string. Nothing downstream ranks, dedupes or filters on them. At roughly 60 characters per claim they are not free, because this block shares a fixed input budget with the recent findings: a device run with 13 claims trimmed the findings from 1622 characters to **430** and produced a 258-character answer covering two neurotransmitters when the evidence supported more. The bank now sends the claim and its source markers only, returning roughly 780 characters to the findings block. The fields remain on `ScoredFact`, since they are still computed during extraction; they simply stop travelling to the model. `[evidence_level: device_verified_for_the_defect+build_verified+test_verified, confidence: high, evidence_source: Maximum device trace, "Supplementary findings trimmed to fit context: 1622 → 430 chars", 13 claims]`
- **[UI]** The What's New sheet is dismissible for good, reachable afterwards, and no longer reads like a changelog. Four problems with the version added in `eee44a4`. **Dismissal did not stick:** `markSeen()` ran only from the Done button, so swiping the sheet away, how most people close one, left the seen state unrecorded and the sheet returned on the next launch. It now marks seen from the sheet's `onDismiss`, which covers every route out. **No way back:** `releaseForCurrentVersion()` existed and was wired to nothing. **`AboutView` already had a "Latest Update" section** with three hardcoded highlights that had gone stale, describing an older release ("Quality modes are easier to scan") while the app was on 4.8; it now renders from `WhatsNewStore.releases`, which removes the second source of release copy and gives the sheet a permanent home. **The copy was changelog prose:** six items averaging 190 characters of engineering explanation, including "Retrieval was never the problem", which means nothing to a user who never knew there was one. Now five items averaging 54 characters, benefit first. `[evidence_level: build_verified, confidence: high, evidence_source: WhatsNewStore.swift, ContentView.swift, AboutView.swift]`
- **[Ingestion]** **Re-importing a document now un-deletes it.** `deleted_documents.json` was written in two places and cleared in none, so a document id that entered it stayed there permanently, and `WorkspaceSyncService` filters every tombstoned id out of `documents_metadata.json` on each sync. Combined with ingestion skipping a file it recognises as already imported, that formed a trap with no exit: a document is deleted, or its queued item is discarded, which calls `removeDocument`, and its id is tombstoned; re-importing finds the surviving record, logs "Skipping already imported document" and creates no new id; the next sync applies the tombstone and erases the record; and the cycle repeats forever, because import always skips and sync always deletes. The user-visible symptom was documents completing an import, sitting fine for minutes, then vanishing on the next launch with a resume prompt for whichever queue item outlived them. Deliberately importing a file is an un-delete, so the skip path now clears any tombstone for that id. `[evidence_level: device_verified_for_the_symptom+code_verified_for_the_cause+build_verified, confidence: high, evidence_source: WorkspaceSyncService.swift:994-1004 and 1075, RAGService.swift registerDeletedDocuments with no clearing path]`
- **[Ingestion]** Completion is judged from document metadata rather than a lazily-loaded vector store. The guard that drops a persisted queue item queried `vectorRouter.db(for:).allChunks()`, but that store is opened per container on demand, and a store that has not been opened answers *empty* without raising an error, so there was nothing for the previous fix's `do`/`catch` to catch. A device log shows the ordering plainly: `Loaded 2 documents (196 chunks)` at line 16, `[BNNS] Loaded 182 chunks` for one container at line 60, the guard reporting chunks missing at line 106, and that document's own `[BNNS] Persisted 13 chunks` arriving at line 400. The ingestion had completed; the store simply was not open yet when the guard ran, so every relaunch resurrected a finished import. `Document.totalChunks` is written at completion and loaded synchronously at launch, it is the same figure that startup line sums, so it is now the primary signal, with the store consulted only when metadata itself reports zero chunks. `[evidence_level: device_verified, confidence: high, evidence_source: iCloud device log lines 16, 60, 106, 400]`
- **[Ingestion]** "Resume interrupted upload?" no longer appears for documents that finished importing. The guard that drops a persisted queue item when its document is already present read the vector store as `(try? await database.allChunks()) ?? []`, collapsing a failed read and a genuinely empty store into the same value, and this branch treats empty as "never ingested". A device log shows the consequence directly: four consecutive `[BNNS] Loaded 182 chunks` lines immediately precede the guard reporting that the same document's chunks are missing. Nothing had been deleted; the lookup returned nothing while the store demonstrably held the data, and the same document was correctly dropped twice earlier in that same log. The read is now a real `do`/`catch`: a failure drops the queued item rather than re-ingesting a document that may already be complete, since a read error is not evidence of a missing document. When zero chunks genuinely match, the warning now names the document id, the container id, the total chunk count and the number of distinct documents in that store, and states plainly whether the store was empty (a real incomplete ingestion) or populated (an id or container mismatch). `[evidence_level: device_verified, confidence: high_for_the_defect_cause_not_yet_isolated, evidence_source: iCloud device log, lines 326 and 454 drop the item, line 614 retains it, preceded by 182 chunks loaded]`
- **[Orchestration]** Citation verification accepts parenthesised markers. `verifyCitations` matched `[S(\d+)]` only, and models routinely write `(S1)` instead of `[S1]` regardless of what the prompt asks for. A device run produced an answer carrying five `(S1)`/`(S2)` markers and the check scored it **`citations=0/0`**, reporting a fully attributed answer as having no sources at all, which then depressed the calibrated confidence that verification feeds. The pattern now accepts both forms, since the difference is punctuation preference rather than grounding. `[evidence_level: device_verified_for_the_defect+build_verified+test_verified, confidence: high, evidence_source: Maximum device trace, 5 paren markers scored 0/0]`
- **[UI]** The What's New sheet is dismissible for good, and reachable afterwards. Three problems with the sheet added earlier in v4.8. Marking a release as seen ran only from the Done button, so swiping the sheet away, how most people close one, never recorded it and the sheet returned on the next launch; it now marks seen from the sheet's own `onDismiss`, covering every exit. `releaseForCurrentVersion()` was dead code with no caller, so a dismissed sheet was gone for good; `AboutView`'s existing "Latest Update" section now renders from `WhatsNewStore.releases`, which also retires three hardcoded highlights that had gone stale describing an older release. And the copy itself read as a changelog rather than a release note: six items averaging 190 characters of engineering explanation, including "Retrieval was never the problem", which is meaningless to anyone who never knew there was one. Now five items averaging 54 characters, benefit first. `[evidence_level: build_verified+test_verified, confidence: high, evidence_source: WhatsNewStore.swift, WhatsNewView.swift, ContentView.swift, AboutView.swift]`
- **[UI]** Added a What's New sheet that appears once after an update. `WhatsNewStore` compares `CFBundleShortVersionString` against a stored `whatsNew.lastSeenVersion` and presents `WhatsNewView` when they differ. Deliberately silent on a fresh install: a first-time user has no "before" to compare against and the onboarding checklist already owns that moment, so the first launch records the version and shows nothing, the sheet appears on the first *update*. Also silent when a build ships without authored copy, recording the version rather than presenting an empty sheet, and skipped entirely in screenshot mode. Release copy lives in Swift rather than a bundled file for the same reason `SampleDocumentManager`'s samples do: it ships with the binary, cannot go missing, and cannot drift from the version it describes. The v4.8 entry reuses the wording from `Docs/USER_CHANGELOG.md` so the store listing, the repo changelog and the in-app sheet all say the same thing. `[evidence_level: build_verified+test_verified, confidence: high, evidence_source: WhatsNewStore.swift, WhatsNewView.swift, ContentView.swift]`
- **[Orchestration]** The synthesis output reserve no longer starves the evidence it protects. `37c2c63` sized the input against the context window but reserved **1500 output tokens**, and a device run used only ~362 of them while the reservation squeezed the supplementary findings from 1554 characters to **166**, thirty-two sessions of insight compressed into two sentences. The answer showed exactly that shape: Dopamine, Glutamate and γ-aminobutyric acid written out in full, then `**Serotonin**` followed by the single line "Document [S2] mentions serotonin." and nothing more, because there was no material left to write from. It was briefly a stop condition and that was wrong, measured across three device runs on the same corpus and question: no convergence at all gave 50 sessions and a 48-character answer with zero citations; saturation-based convergence gave 23 sessions, a 209-character answer with 1 of 2 citations verified and Self-RAG `accept` at 76%; the coverage-plateau rule gave 8 sessions, a 67-character answer with **0 of 1** citations verified and Self-RAG **`retry`** at 57%. Novelty stayed at 61–86% throughout because the chain was still accumulating *depth*: further supporting evidence for the sub-questions already covered, which is precisely what let synthesis produce a citation the verifier could check. - **[Orchestration]** Reverted the relevance-head session ceiling from `c2ec120`; it was wrong. That change budgeted Maximum for only the top 60% of the relevance-ordered pool, reasoning that late sessions read the weakest chunks. The device trace it cited refutes it: sessions 16 through 22 ran at **66–86% novelty**, and the chain stopped at 23 on *saturation* with `lowNoveltyStreak=0`, it was still finding new material when it quit. A 15-session ceiling would have amputated seven productive sessions. The ceiling is restored to cover the retrieved pool. The correct division of labour, recorded so this is not re-litigated: **retrieval** decides what is relevant and does so with inspectable scores; **convergence** decides when reasoning has stopped paying, and it demonstrably works; the **ceiling** exists only to stop a pathological run. A flat percentage is neither a score nor an earned stop, so it belongs to no stage. Note this does not contradict RAG's premise, not reading 100% of the *corpus* is the entire point of retrieval, and the app's own product copy says so. - **[Orchestration]** Maximum stops when it has stopped learning, and its budget scales with the evidence. A device run spent **26 minutes and 50 sessions** on a single-document library of 181 chunks, with novelty at 50% and saturation at 90% from session 40 onward, the chain knew it was finished and kept going. Cause: the stop condition required `confidence >= 0.98` **and** coverage **and** novelty exhaustion simultaneously, and 0.98 is unreachable. `sessionProgress` caps at 0.68 and the remainder must come from coverage and novelty terms *minus a saturation penalty that grows as the chain matures*, so high novelty late means you have not saturated while low novelty means the penalty cancels the gain. Runs peaked at 89%. This is the same shape as Deep Think's 73%-ceiling-versus-90%-gate fixed in `8a8c7ab`. Notably `noveltyExhausted` was already a correct marginal-gain stopping rule, streak-based patience over low novelty, high saturation, or 85% source coverage, it was simply gated behind a number it could never reach. The condition is now `completed || converged` rather than a three-way conjunction, with a `minimumSessionsBeforeConvergence` floor so a cold start is not mistaken for exhaustion. Worse, the recursive-research fallback it triggered was accepted on one condition only: that the replacement was not *itself* flagged as a miss. No such tool or schema exists in the codebase and tools are disabled for chain sessions, so the phrasing was priming a structured response. Contamination cascaded, since each insight becomes the next session's `PRIOR FINDINGS`. The middle-session prompt no longer names a field and states plain-prose output explicitly; a generic envelope stripper salvages the prose as a safety net rather than discarding the session's work. `[evidence_level: device_verified, confidence: high, evidence_source: A/B on identical corpus, 3 of 8 sessions emitting JSON before, 0 of 8 after]`

> Opened the same day iOS 4.7 / macOS 3.0 went out (and were approved), so this
> instrumentation work rides the next version. Builds 200+ carry the 4.8 stamp.

### Added
- **[Orchestration]** Instrumented the pipeline with `OSSignposter` stage boundaries (Phase A of the benchmark plan). New `PipelineSignposts` utility exposes `Ingestion`/`Query`/`Synthesis` categories under subsystem `Gunndamental.OpenIntelligence.Pipeline`, with twelve intervals at the load-bearing seams: document processing, hybrid search, context assembly, execution planning, generation, verification gates, the agentic umbrella, and each synthesis strategy (direct/comprehensive/honest/decomposed). Near-zero cost when no recorder is attached; ships in release builds so `xcrun xctrace` can attribute wall-clock per stage per quality mode on physical hardware. `[evidence_level: build_verified+test_verified, confidence: exact_for_simulator, evidence_source: PipelineSignposts.swift and the seven instrumented services]`
- **[Orchestration]** Resolved receipt-fidelity findings F-06/F-07. F-06: partial streams now record a `.partial` attempt result instead of `.failed`, the delivered text genuinely came from that route, so `.partial` attests the `completedTarget` in `RouteEvalMetrics` while staying distinguishable from a clean completion; metrics gain a `partialCompletions` count. F-07 closed with **no code change**: tracing `actualRoute` showed it is fixed at session construction, so LLMService's one-element chains describe construction-time fallbacks where the intended route was never attempted (truthful as-is), and runtime PCC failures already merge the two-element chain in `RAGService`. `[evidence_level: code_verified+test_verified, confidence: high, evidence_source: ModelExecutionReceipt.swift, RAGService.swift partial-stream path, RouteEvalMetrics.swift, RouteEvalMetricsTests.swift, ensureSession trace]`

## 4.7 - 2026-07-28

> Shipped to the App Store as iOS 4.7 / macOS 3.0 on 2026-07-28 (build 199).
> A small, deliberate release: the 4.6 binary had already shipped the PCC
> routing work, so 4.7 is the honesty-and-instrumentation pass on top of it.

### Added
- **[Retrieval]** Added `Benchmarks/rag_eval_v1.jsonl`, the first dataset the documented `RAGEvalRunner`/`RAGEvalMetrics` quality gates can actually run against. `Docs/EVALS.md` had specified a JSONL corpus and `RAGEvalDataset.load(from:)` had expected one, but no such file existed anywhere in the repository, so every documented gate was unrunnable. The dataset is generated by `scripts/build_eval_dataset.py` from the existing `tiny_research_suite` manifest rather than hand-authored, so the in-app validation harness and the formal gate framework cannot drift apart; `--check` fails if the committed file is stale. 20 cases over committed synthetic fixtures (no private content): 5 exact-value, 5 single-document recall, 3 lost-in-middle position sensitivity, 5 two-hop multi-document synthesis, and 2 negative controls that must abstain. `RAGEvalDatasetSchemaTests` pins the decoding contract so a `RAGEvalCase` change cannot silently invalidate the corpus. `[evidence_level: code_verified+test_verified, confidence: high, evidence_source: Benchmarks/rag_eval_v1.jsonl, scripts/build_eval_dataset.py, RAGEvalDatasetSchemaTests.swift]`
- **[Orchestration]** Native Private Cloud Compute execution is confirmed working on a physical iOS 27 device running v4.6, reported by the owner from direct device testing. This clears the central open question for the v4.6 PCC work, that PCC actually executes rather than silently falling back on-device, and supersedes the prior blanket "code-verified, unverified for device" status for the execution path. Not covered by that confirmation and still unverified: quota exhaustion, mid-stream network-transition fallback, background/App Intent consent behavior, and Archive/TestFlight distribution signatures. `[evidence_level: user_confirmed, confidence: high_for_execution_path_unverified_for_edge_scenarios, evidence_source: owner device testing on v4.6, 2026-07-28]`
- **[Orchestration]** Added Phase 9 route-evidence gates in `RouteEvalMetrics`. `RAGEvalMetrics` scores answer quality; the new type scores route honesty by checking six invariants over `ModelExecutionReceipt` chains, a completed route must have a succeeded attempt on that route, intent/outcome divergence must carry a fallback reason (and only then), non-authorizing quota states (`limitReached`, `unsupported`, `unknown`) must block PCC attempts, non-abstaining receipts must record an attempt chain, and the attempted route must appear in it. Also reports per-route completion latency, PCC→local fallback counts, and a markdown evidence summary. An empty run fails the gates: absence of receipts is not evidence of correctness. The gates are computable from receipts alone, so identical scoring applies to simulator, physical-device, and TestFlight runs, this makes device evidence checkable, it does not substitute for it. `[evidence_level: code_verified+test_verified, confidence: high, evidence_source: RouteEvalMetrics.swift, RouteEvalMetricsTests.swift]`
- **[General]** Added `Docs/AUDIT/ROADMAP_RECONCILIATION_2026-07-28.md`, reconciling `Docs/ROADMAP.md`, this changelog, and both Notion roadmap databases against current `main`. Records seven findings, corrects the Notion status of CoreSpotlight indexing and Translation.framework (both shipped and wired but tracked as not-started/in-progress), and identifies the public Notion database as containing no v4.5 or v4.6 work at all. `[evidence_level: code_verified, confidence: high, evidence_source: Docs/AUDIT/ROADMAP_RECONCILIATION_2026-07-28.md]`

### Fixed
- **[Orchestration]** Hardened `ci_scripts/ci_post_clone.sh` against the failure that broke the Xcode Cloud build on 2026-07-28. The script derives `MARKETING_VERSION` from the first `## <number>` heading in `CHANGELOG.md`; because `[Unreleased]` had accumulated a release worth of entries while the first numbered heading below it was still the already-shipped 4.6, CI silently re-stamped a released version on every run. The script now refuses to build when `[Unreleased]` contains entries, explaining that the heading below it describes an already-cut version and printing the exact fix. It also verifies the substitution applied and echoes the iOS and macOS versions together, so a mismatched pair is visible in the build log rather than discovered at upload. Verified by replaying both the current tree (passes, 4.7 / 3.0) and a reconstruction of the failing state (blocks). `[evidence_level: code_verified+test_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: ci_scripts/ci_post_clone.sh, replay of both states 2026-07-28]`
- **[UI]** Removed the last user-facing 3B/20B parameter-count claims, completing the v4.6 "Public Model Truth" work that had corrected execution and telemetry but left the presentation layer asserting the old capability. The Settings capability card no longer advertises "Dynamic routing between on-device 3B Core, 20B Core Advanced, and massive PCC models," a `3B / 20B` chip, a `4K / 4K / 32K, 3B / 20B / PCC` context stat, or a footer promising scaling "from 3B parameters to massive PCC clusters"; `ModelInfoCard`, `UnifiedMetricsBar`, and the container settings sheet no longer name a parameter count either. Re-verified against the current SDK before changing anything: a live `swiftc -typecheck` probe against `iPhoneSimulator27.0.sdk` reports `type 'SystemLanguageModel' has no member 'advanced'`; the public interface exposes only `availability`, `isAvailable`, and `UseCase{.general, .contentTagging}`, a task selector, not a size selector; `FoundationModelSessionFactory.swift:56-72` runs `SystemLanguageModel.default` for `.onDeviceAdvanced` on every device; and the `physicalMemory >= 11.5GB` gate in `DeviceHardware.supportsAdvancedOnDeviceModel` has zero call sites, so nothing in the app selects a larger model by RAM. (Owner-prompted WWDC26 research the same day confirmed AFM 3 Core Advanced, a 20B sparse on-device model, is real at the OS level, "unlocked by and optimized for our most capable Apple silicon systems"; it remains OS-managed with no developer selection or observation API in the installed SDK, so the label removal stands with refined reasoning: the model exists, the app's claimed control over it did not. A CI canary now alerts if Apple exposes selection.) `[evidence_level: sdk_verified+code_verified+build_verified, confidence: exact, evidence_source: swiftc probe 2026-07-28, FoundationModels.swiftinterface, LLMModelType.swift, ModelInfoCard.swift, UnifiedMetricsBar.swift, ContainerSettingsSheet+Sections.swift]`
- **[Chunking]** Made entity extraction deterministic. The capitalized-entity supplement added earlier in v4.6 was gated on `entities.isEmpty`, so it only ran when *every* Natural Language pass failed, never on partial results. Because NER tags "Apple" in "Tim Cook works at Apple in Cupertino." as `.otherWord` rather than an organization, that token was only ever captured by the lexical-class pass, and that pass does not classify it consistently across process states. The same chunk therefore produced different entities between runs, which propagates into the entity index and 2-hop graph expansion. The supplement now runs unconditionally. An intermediate fix that gated on "did each pass emit any tag" was tried and rejected: a pass can emit tags while still missing the token, so a present-but-differently-classified token is indistinguishable from an absent one at that layer. Cost is bounded, the supplement only appends, dedupes against the existing set, and the final `prefix(15)` means it can contribute only when the earlier passes returned fewer than 15 entities. Caught by `testExtractEntitiesNamedEntities` passing in isolation, then failing intermittently across full-suite runs. `[evidence_level: code_verified+test_verified, confidence: high, evidence_source: SemanticChunker.swift extractEntities, repeated full-suite runs 2026-07-28]`

Test counts harmonized to the verified 147/147. - **[General]** Root workspace cleanup, evidence-preserving. Console dumps formerly tracked at the root (`macosconsole.txt`, `THis.md`, `DeepThinkConsole&Trace.txt`, `youtube_videos.json`) were removed from HEAD, they remain in git history (last present at `81073c9`) with local copies in the untracked `.attic.nosync/`. Historical one-off reports moved to `Docs/Archive/` with a provenance README, since governance documents cite them as evidence; `PCC_Dynamic_Routing_Audit_Spec.md` moved to `Docs/`. All untracked personal/marketing artifacts (including 4 GB of demo screen recordings and a `google-ads.yaml` containing credentials) moved to `.attic.nosync/`, which is gitignored and excluded from iCloud sync. The stale `integration/safe-merge-candidate` stash was preserved as branch `wip/deep-think-live-steps` before dropping, it contains Deep Think live-step UI work that is only partially landed on main. No app source changed. `[evidence_level: code_verified, confidence: exact]`

The overlay now always maps against the full screen.

Consolidates audited PRs #27 and #55; neither branch was merged directly.

Distribution builds can no longer instantiate native Private Cloud Compute without a provable entitlement; the existing safe fallback path engages instead.

Supersedes audited PR #34, whose literal patch would have changed tab handling.
